War Dogs of History: Sergeant Stubby and the Birth of the American War Dog Legend

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

We just sent a welcome email your way with a quick overview of what to expect (including those vital recall alerts).

Dogster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn a commission at no cost to you. Read about privacy.

Long before the United States military established an official war dog program, one small stray taught the Army what a dog could do in combat. His name was Stubby, and by the time the First World War ended, he had become the most decorated dog in American military history.

Stubbyâs story began in the summer of 1917 at Yale Field in New Haven, Connecticut, where the 102nd Infantry Regiment of the 26th âYankeeâ Division was training before deployment to France. A short-tailed, brindle-colored dog of uncertain breeding, likely part Boston terrier and part American bull terrier, wandered into camp and was adopted by Private J. Robert Conroy. Soldiers were not permitted to keep pets, but Stubby won over the men, and eventually the officers, by learning to mimic military customs. He reportedly marched in step with the troops and raised a paw to his brow in a passable salute.

When the 102nd Infantry shipped out for France, the transport ships barred dogs. Conroy smuggled Stubby aboard the SS Minnesota hidden beneath his overcoat. Conroyâs commanding officer discovered Stubby during the crossing, but his salute reportedly charmed the officer into letting him stay.

Once in France, Stubbyâs role shifted from morale booster to something closer to that of a working soldier. He entered combat on February 5, 1918, at Chemin des Dames, enduring shelling for over a month, and went on to serve through 17 battles over 18 months on the Western Front.

After surviving a mustard gas attack, Stubby developed a heightened sensitivity to the smell of gas. Historians and contemporary newspaper accounts credit him with warning his regiment of subsequent gas attacks before the men could detect it themselves. This allowed soldiers time to don their masks. He was also credited with locating wounded soldiers in no manâs land and comforting them until stretcher bearers arrived.

Stubbyâs most famous exploit occurred when he discovered a German soldier mapping the layout of Allied trenches. According to accounts preserved by military historians, Stubby seized the man until American soldiers arrived to take him prisoner. For this act, Stubby was informally promoted to the rank of sergeant, making him, according to most accounts, the first dog to receive a rank in the United States Armed Forces. This was, of course, an honorary and unofficial designation rather than a formal commission, since the military does not commission dogs.

During the April 1918 German offensive on the town of Seicheprey, France, Stubby was wounded in the chest and leg by grenade shrapnel while accompanying the 102nd Infantry in combat. After recovering, he returned to visiting wounded soldiers in the hospital, walking the wards to lift the spirits of recovering men.

Stubby returned home a celebrity. He led parades, attended war bond rallies, and met three sitting or future presidents: Woodrow Wilson, Warren G. Harding, and Calvin Coolidge. General John J. Pershing personally honored him at a 1921 ceremony for the 102nd Infantry Regiment, presenting him with a gold medal from the Humane Education Society, an award that, while not an official military commendation, cemented his reputation as the greatest war dog of his era.

Conroy went on to attend Georgetown University Law Center, and Stubby went with him, becoming the unofficial mascot of the Georgetown Hoyas football team. At halftime, he would nudge a football around the field to the delight of the crowd.

Stubby died in 1929, reportedly in Conroyâs arms. His remains were preserved and are held today by the Smithsonianâs National Museum of American History, where he wears the medals and blanket he wore during his years of service. In 2018, an animated film called Sgt. Stubby: An American Hero introduced his story to a new generation. That same year, the descendants of Conroy dedicated a life-sized bronze statue of him at the Connecticut Trees of Honor Memorial in Middletown, Connecticut.

Stubby served nearly a quarter century before the United States Army established a formal War Dog Program in 1942. He had no training pipeline, no breeding program, and no structure to recognize what he did. He was, in the truest sense, an amateur who became indispensable through instinct, loyalty, and repeated exposure to danger alongside the men who fed and sheltered him. Later advocates of formal military working dog programs often cite his legacy as early proof of concept: a dogâs senses, courage, and bond with a handler could save human lives on a modern battlefield.

Every military working dog story, whether it unfolded in the trenches of World War I or on a patrol in Afghanistan, is also the story of a partnership. Learning that history is one of the simplest ways to honor the generations of dogs and handlers who served side by side. If these stories have moved you, there are meaningful ways to help carry them forward.

Support retired dogs. Not every military working dog can retire to a family couch. The Ddamien Project provides lifelong homes for retired military, contract, and law enforcement dogs whose medical or behavioral needs make typical adoption difficult.

Learn more. The Military Working Dog Heritage Museum, founded by former K9 handlers, is gathering the photographs, documents, and firsthand accounts of the dogs and people who served. Its newsletter, The Paw Print, shares many of these stories with the public.

Keep today's working dogs strong. Canine Advancement Group, led by veterinarian Dr. Brian Farr, advocates for treating working dogs as the athletes they are, offering evidence-based guidance on fitness, welfare, and handler education.

Follow the national tribute. The National Service Animals Monument, planned for Washington, DC, will honor service animals and their handlers across military, law enforcement, and assistance roles, including generations of military working dogs.

These dogs are part of our history. By preserving their stories, honoring their service, and caring for the dogs still serving and those who have come home, we help make sure their legacy endures.

If Sergeant Stubby proved what an untrained stray could do in the First World War, Chips proved what a dog could do once the United States built an actual program around him. Chips became the most decorated American dog of World War II. His story also marks the moment the Army decided it should never again give such awards to an animal.

We just sent a welcome email your way with a quick overview of what to expect (including those vital recall alerts).

Morgan Farr is a writer, graphic designer, and advocate who has spent her career telling the stories of working dogs and the people who love them. She serves as the Social Media Coordinator for both the Military Working Dog Heritage Museum and the National Service Animals Monument. She previously served as the social media manager for the U.S. Army Military Working Dog Program.

Together with her husband, an Army veterinarian, Morgan co-founded Canine Advancement Group, which offers education on canine wellness and welfare for both working and companion dogs. She is a member of the American Veterinary Medical History Society, the American Historical Association, the American Association for State and Local History, and the Society for Military History. In 2025, she was named the Armed Forces Insurance Military Spouse of the Year for Joint Base San Antonio.

At home, Morgan is a military spouse, a homeschooling mother of four, a small business owner of Dog-Eared Press, and a co-op instructor. She shares her household with a high-drive Dutch Shepherd and a spirited Bombay cat, who remind her daily that every animal, working or companion, deserves understanding, partnership, and thoughtful care.

You can find her across social media platforms using her handle @morgandfarrauthor.

Experts update prostate cancer screening guidelines for men

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

Disability in Health Care Reporting Fellow

Rose covers disability and mental health, and she helps write STAT’s daily Morning Rounds newsletter. Confidential tips can be sent to Rose on Signal at rosebroderick.11.

Get your daily dose of health and medicine every weekday with STAT’s free newsletter Morning Rounds. Sign up here.

Happy Thursday. We’ve got a great lineup for you today, including an inside look at shuffles within the Senate’s health committee, and what it might mean for “Medicare for All.”

The American Cancer Society released a major update to its guidelines today, recommending testing for prostate-specific antigen blood levels every two years starting at age 50 for men with an average risk of prostate cancer. Men in high-risk groups, like Black men and men with a significant history of prostate cancer in the family, are urged to get tested annually starting at 45. The guideline’s authors hope that increased surveillance will reduce the number of Black patients presenting with cancers at advanced stages, as this demographic is disproportionately represented in prostate cancer mortality.

The changes reflect recent findings that screening through PSA levels reduced disease-specific mortality for certain men. It had fallen out of favor for its lack of specificity before recent findings returned it to specialists’ good graces. Read more from STAT’s Annalisa Merelli.

The Senate health committee is in the spotlight once again.

Health, Education, Labor and Pensions Committee members announced they will vote next week on whether to nominate Heidi Overton as commissioner of the Food and Drug Administration. Republicans are still in power in this lame duck session, but Overton’s approval is not a given, STAT’s Margaret Manto explains, since a key Republican hasn’t said whether he backs her.

And colleague John Wilkerson noted Tuesday that Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-Vt.) plans to move the party towards supporting “Medicare for All” once he becomes chair of the committee in January. But he’ll have a new Republican colleague to spar with.

Sen. Rand Paul (R-Ky.) announced yesterday that he will forgo his leadership of the Senate homeland security committee to join HELP as the top Republican next year. Why is this bureaucratic shuffling noteworthy? The senator used the homeland security committee’s unusually powerful subpoena authority to accuse Anthony Fauci, without evidence, of conspiring to cover up the origins of the Covid-19 pandemic. Committee members voted along party lines to hold the former National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases director in contempt of Congress.

Moving to the health committee will allow Rand to fight with Sanders over Medicare for All and all manner of other health issues.

(Most of the information about Paul came from John Wilkerson and our D.C. Diagnosis newsletter. If you want puns and great updates about which senators STAT reporters are chasing down in the bowels of Congress, you can sign up here.)

Body mass index is a flawed measure for obesity, but it remains deeply entrenched in global medical systems. If the world replaced it, obesity rates would increase and millions of people’s obesity diagnoses could flip, according to a new study.

Experts took a framework developed last year that offers a new understanding of how weight can impact a person’s health outcomes and applied it to obesity rates in the United States, the United Kingdom, China, and Spain. After analyzing 844,000 participants, the researchers calculated that BMI-based obesity missed 6.2% of adults compared to the new metric.

The measure ditches BMI’s focus on a person’s height and weight to include where fat is stored in a person’s body, most notably abdominal fat. It could have major implications for accurately assessing the mortality risk from obesity. Read more about the study from STAT’s Andrew Joseph.

Two new First Opinion pieces explore how the movement’s history informs its present. In one, professor Jerel Ezell argues that MAHA has coopted strategies from Black environmentalists fighting systemic health and food system fissures for decades. “Even as Kennedy and MAHA have cribbed from the Black activist playbook, they’ve actively undermined” Black Americans, he writes.

In another, professor Gavin Yamey argues that it’s not surprising key leaders are standing by convicted rapist Russell Brand, given the movement’s history of cozying up to violent men. “There is a rot of toxic masculinity and violence at MAHA’s core,” Yamey writes. Read more.

If you’ve ever spent months cycling through different doses of the same drug to find one that works, it could be your pharmacogenes at work. This set of genes affect how your body responds to prescription drugs, impacting what dose works for you and potential side effects.

Scientists have just identified thousands of new variants in these genes, thanks to a study using data from nearly 100,000 Americans through the National Institute of Health’s All of Us program. The advances are partly due to the program’s collection of genetic data from diverse backgrounds.

The study found that every participant carried at least one variant in the 16 pharmacogenes they studied, and that one in four participants had taken a medication that could be affected by these variants — everything from selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors to chemotherapy. Anil Oza has the write up.

Rush Double Down on 2027 Sphere Residency Run Due to ‘Incredible Demand’: See the New Dates

The group's Elevate From the Norm stay-put is up to 10 shows now.

Rush just doubled down on their 2027 Las Vegas Sphere residency. After initially announcing a five-show run as part of their rapturously received reunion tour last week, the Canadian prog rock legends announced an additional five shows on Wednesday (Nov. 11). Explore Rush See latest videos, charts and news

According to an Instagram post, the group said the Elevate From the Norm residency will now feature a handful of shows in June as well. “WOW! Thank you everyone ❤️🙏 Due to incredible demand, FIVE NEW SHOWS have been added to RUSH live at Sphere Las Vegas!,” they wrote. The newly added shows will be on June 15, 17, 19, 24 and 26. They will join the originally announced dates — May 21, 23, 26, 28, 30 —  from the group led by co-founders bassist/singer Geddy Lee and guitarist Alex Lifeson that will bring the “Tom Sawyer” group to the immersive venue for the first time.

A pre-sale for all tickets, VIP and travel packages for the new dates is available now through 10 p.m. PT on Thursday (Nov. 12); click here ticketing information.

Each night of the residency will feature a set of fan favorites and greatest hits, in addition to the touching tributes the band has been doing to late drummer/lyricist Neil Peart, who died on Jan. 7, 2020.

The group kicked off their first tour in 11 years — and first without Peart — on June 7 of this year and they are scheduled to wind it down with an April 10 show in Helsinki, Finland before setting up shop in Vegas.

The latest FreeBSD report is here, and the AI debate is ongoing

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Only a minority want to ban it. Plus plenty of updates on 16 projects

The FreeBSD project's status team has published FreeBSD's third status report of 2026, showing plenty of ambitious work underway and a community still undecided on how to treat code from AI coding assistants.

The FreeBSD Status Report Third Quarter 2026 is a substantial document, with 51 entries from five teams covering 16 projects, plus six other sections with more detailed reports.

There are updates on the progress of the Sylve project, which is an attempt to bring a smooth polished GUI for managing FreeBSD virtualization hosts – something akin to Proxmox for FreeBSD. We mentioned this when we looked at the new Daemonarchy project last month. Another covers the new AppJail effort – a containerized app distribution system for FreeBSD, including for GUI apps, which aims for something broadly comparable to Snap, Flatpak and AppImage on Linux.

In other words, interesting and quite radical stuff is happening in the FreeBSD space.

What this particular LLM-skeptical vulture was most interested to see, though, was an update on the internal discussion about how to handle code generated by AI coding assistants in the project. We first reported on this vexed subject back in September 2025 and no final answer has yet been published.

NetBSD banned AI-generated code in 2024 and although the bot-boosters say this will doom it to irrelevance, as we reported last month, the project is thriving. Also back in October, we looked at OpenBSD 8.0, whose launch materials are full of mockery for AI users.

Under the FreeBSD Core Team section, there is a subsection headed "FreeBSD Developer AI survey." The survey was created by Baptiste Daroussin, and received 140 responses. Although the percentages show that there is some resistance to AI, there are hints here as to why it’s taking so long. Some project committers are against it, yet many more are proponents. Since there are only seven bullet points, we thought we would quote them in full:

Policy: 88 percent want the Project to adopt a policy, either a formal, written policy (49 percent) or light guidance and best practices (39 percent). Six  percent said that AI should be explicitly discouraged, and three percent that the existing contribution rules are sufficient.

AI-assisted code: 35  percent said it must be disclosed, 25 percent that it must be reviewed more strictly than human code, 22 percent that it should be treated like any other contribution, and 16 percent that it should be rejected outright.

Safeguards: mandatory human review (78 percent) and mandatory disclosure (69 percent) were requested most often, followed by provenance or licensing documentation (47 percent) and mandatory testing (39 percent).

Disclosure: 84 percent want AI use disclosed, either always (48 percent) or when its involvement is significant (36 percent). 79 percent want AI-assisted commits marked in the commit log, and 56 percent prefer a tag that names the tool.

Concerns: the main concerns about quality are correctness and subtle bugs (39 percent, licensing and provenance (26 percent), and maintainability (21 percent). 75 percent are somewhat or very concerned about copyright and licensing.

Principles: accountability, meaning that a human remains responsible (91 percent), and transparency (78 percent) were selected most often, and preserving code quality was the top-ranked goal for a policy.

Other uses: 65 percent would allow AI-generated documentation with human review. 54 percent would allow AI tools on Project infrastructure, most of them with restrictions, and 29 percent would not.

Only six percent for explicit discouragement, 16 percent for outright rejection,  and 35 percent against LLM-generated documentation. This plus the usual sort of calls for responsible use and disclosure, concerns about copyright issues, and so on.

The Reg is strongly reminded of the Debian developer poll we reported on in August. This had no fewer than four slightly different AI-refusal options out of eight, and despite the Condorcet voting method it uses, the result was giving permission to use bots.

With such relatively weak opposition, we suspect that FreeBSD will go the same way as Debian: tell developers to be careful, but allow bot usage, resulting in LLM-extruded code being committed into the project. ®

Mauricio Pochettino will ditch four Man Utd stars – the winners and losers under new boss

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino is poised to take the reins at Manchester United in the club’s latest attempt to reset the clock.

Having been regarded as a genuine candidate for the job at Old Trafford on three occasions in the past, the Argentine now gets his chance in Manchester where United’s situation is more dire than ever.

With Michael Carrick’s exit confirmed, Pochettino will inherit a squad that has been routinely booed off the pitch by a fanbase thoroughly disenchanted with a lack of effort on it. While there may be hopes of recruitment in the January transfer window, Pochettino will have to work with what he has at his disposal until then.

Metro looks across the United squad to identify whose prospects look brighter than ever with the new manager en route – and those who face the axe.

Read exclusive analysis of the biggest stories in football with Metro's weekly football newsletter In The Mixer. Sign up now for free.

With Benjamin Sesko starting just two matches this season before injury struck again, Bryan Mbeumo has spent most of the campaign operating up top. He could remain in that role until Sesko is fit again but both he and Matheus Cunha stand to benefit in their natural positions under the new regime.

Pochettino has favoured inverted wingers to help create overloads in the middle with Cunha in particular tailor made for that role.

Moussa Dembele evolved into one of the Premier League’s most dominant midfielders at Tottenham under Pochettino, carrying on the work started by Martin Jol at Fulham with the Belgian. Kobbie Mainoo lacks Dembele’s outstanding athleticism but his press resistance and running power with the ball will be regarded as key assets for Pochettino.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

Mainoo does not have the constant high-energy output Pochettino has traditionally demanded of his midfielders but he has improved his engine over the last two years and his ability to break the lines could also see him play a key role.

Erik ten Hag, Ruben Amorim and Michael Carrick all lauded the qualities of Mason Mount despite injuries ensuring they never saw the best of him. Pochettino could be another to look favourably upon him if he can prove his fitness.

When in negotiations with Chelsea back in 2023, Pochettino was eager for the club to end a contract stand-off with the midfielder and was desperate to keep him and make him a key component of his rebuild west London. His own arrival came too late to revive negotiations however with Mount joining United that summer.

Mount’s work rate has never been in doubt and his abilities to run off the ball or intelligently drop into space could be vital. But with just one start in the Premier League this season, the same old doubts are in place.

In October, former United captain Gary Neville called time on the Mainoo-Youri Tielemans partnership, neither dynamic or athletic enough to last the course in the Premier League. There may only be one winner with the Belgian perhaps most at risk.

Tielemans is adept in keeping possession and progressing play but his lack of drive up the pitch is seemingly at odds with how Pochettino likes to set up his midfield. Pochettino was also said to have turned down the chance to sign the midfielder on at least one occasion after his name was put forward by the Tottenham board.

The same concerns for Tielemans could also apply for another summer recruit in Andrey Santos who looks to be another passive operator based on evidence to date.

The emphasis Pochettino puts on his full-backs has been huge during his time in the Premier League but it is the area of the pitch where United are perhaps at their weakest. Diogo Dalot’s shortcomings on the right continue to be exposed while a long-term replacement for Luke Shaw is well overdue.

Dalot’s prospects are brighter – while his final ball is consistently short of the required standard, his ability to get up and down the pitch will be important. Shaw can no longer offer that kind of service on the left and Pochettino may favour the energy of Patrick Dorgu.

Pochettino helped nurture Shaw at Southampton before his £27million move to Manchester in 2014 but the 31-year-old will now struggle to provide the intensity the role demands.

United’s defence has been an utter mess and with the exception of perhaps Leny Yoro, all should consider their futures uncertain. But at 34, Maguire is perhaps the most at risk.

Pochettino favours a high defensive line, requiring centre-halves with pace to live with it. Maguire will turn 34 in March and has been at the heart of a backline that has conceded 19 goals in 10 Premier League games this season.

Pochettino may favour the younger legs of Yoro and Ayden Heaven while Lisandro Martinez’s ability to build from the back could keep him in the picture for now.

Report: PSV in contact with De Bruyne over potential January transfer

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Reports in the Netherlands claim that PSV Eindhoven are interested in signing Kevin De Bruyne from Napoli this winter, and that the Dutch side have been in contact with the Belgian ‘for some time’.

De Bruyne’s time with Napoli could already be coming to an end: Reports in Italy earlier this week claimed that the former Manchester City star could leave the club as early as January and reports over the last 24 hours claim that PSV are among the interested parties.

De Bruyne joined Napoli on a free transfer at the end of his Manchester City contract just shy of 18 months ago, but things haven’t exactly gone to plan for the club or player since then.

The 35-year-old missed over four months of the 2025-26 season with a major hamstring injury, and though Napoli ended last season in the Serie A top four, De Bruyne still found himself wrapped up in controversy when he said that he was ‘happy’ to see Scudetto-winning head coach Antonio Conte leave.

Conte has since been replaced by Massimiliano Allegri, who has, however, gone on to lose seven of his first 15 matches in charge of the Partenopei, leaving them in 10th place in the Serie A table during the November international break and at risk of missing out on qualification to the Champions League knock-out rounds.

De Bruyne is under contract until June 30, although the club has an option to extend that agreement for a further 12 months. There have been recent suggestions in Italy that a departure could be brought forward to January.

Recent reports in the Netherlands claim that PSV are particularly interested in offering De Bruyne a route out of Napoli.

Transfer expert Mounir Boualin claims that PSV have been in contact with De Bruyne’s representatives ‘for some time’ and that discussions have been positive thus far. Local newspaper Eindhovens Dagblad agrees that PSV are interested in De Bruyne, but adds that there is a long way to go before a final agreement is reached.

The Dutch side are not the only club interested in a potential deal for De Bruyne this winter, according to the latest updates.

Curiously, an Instagram post from 433nl reporting PSV’s interest in De Bruyne was liked by Netherlands international Noa Lang, who spent three seasons with the Dutch club and has also been linked with a January move away from Napoli.

Nebius falls 16% as company expects increased 2026 capex spend, issues weak outlook

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Nebius shares sank 16% on Thursday as one of the biggest publicly traded neoclouds reported third-quarter revenue that beat analysts' estimates, but issued disappointing guidance.

Here's how the company performed relative to LSEG consensus:

It was not immediately clear if the loss per share was comparable to the 69 cents expected by analysts.

Revenue jumped 571% in the quarter, according to a statement. The company reported a net loss of $37.5 million, a loss of 13 cents per share, compared with $119.6 million, a loss of 50 cents per share, a year earlier.

The stock move also comes amid the resignation of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, after stinging revelations about multiple extramarital affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy.

Nvidia, which has developed the neocloud market by investing in the companies and backstopping some of their projects, held about $329 million in Nebius shares at the end of June, according to a filing.

In a letter to shareholders, CEO Arkady Volozh said the company expects fiscal year 2026 revenue at the low end of the company's $3 billion to 3.4 billion range. FactSet expected full-year revenue of $3.35 billion.

Nebius also shared an initial outlook for 2027, expecting revenue of $12 billion to $13 billion, "reflecting a conservative approach to the pace of our build-out." FactSet expected 2027 revenue of $12.09 billion.

The company expects FY2026 capital expenditures of $27 billion, higher than its previous capex guidance of $20 billion to $25 billion and the FactSet expectation of $23.85 billion.

Nebius' expected FY2027 capex of $40 billion to $45 billion came in much higher than the FactSet expectation of $28.77 billion.

Nebius' results come after fellow neoclouds CoreWeave and IREN reported better-than-expected financials in their earnings announcements over the past week. That's provided a boost to the companies' stocks, which had suffered steep selloffs in recent weeks on concern about the sustainability of the artificial intelligence buildout.

The neocloud companies posted a robust rally on Wednesday, with Coreweave booming nearly 25%, Nebius climbing 15%, Applied Digital rising more than 7% and IREN adding nearly 6%.

Each of the stocks fell Thursday premarket after the Nebius Q3 report.

Last week, CoreWeave's bond yields spiked past the 16% mark. In its earnings report on Tuesday, CoreWeave told analysts it expects to keep parent-level debt financing under $5 billion through 2027. During the quarter, Nebius said it raised $5.75 billion in debt financing.

The political backdrop has created further volatility as Nebius races rivals to open up new data centers. Last week U.S. voters supported dozens of ballot initiatives to restrict the development of such facilities, while Democrats won sweeping victories in both houses of Congress.

Executives will discuss the results with analysts and issue guidance on a conference call starting at 8 a.m. ET.

Just 3 Major U.S. Metros Are Seller’s Markets–the Fewest Since the Pandemic Freeze

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Just three of the 50 most populous U.S. metro areas were seller’s markets in October. That’s tied for the fewest in records dating back to 2016, matching April 2020, when the pandemic froze the housing market.

Nassau County, NY was the number-one seller’s market, with 24.7% fewer sellers than buyers. The other seller’s markets were Newark, NJ (-14.3%) and Montgomery County, PA (-12.1%).

All three seller’s markets became less strong seller’s markets in October. In Nassau County, there were 28.3% fewer sellers than buyers in September, a bigger gap than October’s 24.7%. Newark’s gap shrunk from 17.6% to 14.3%, and Montgomery County’s shrunk from 20.1% to 12.1%. Milwaukee, which was a seller’s market in September, is now balanced, meaning it doesn’t favor sellers or buyers.

The seller’s markets are places where limited homebuilding has kept housing supply tight. In the greater New York City area, combine that with strong demand for homes near a major job center, and buyers are competing for homes. Home-sale prices rose an average of 3.3% year over year across the three seller’s markets in October. That’s compared with a 0.3% uptick across all of the buyer’s markets, a sign that in seller’s markets, competition among buyers is pushing up home prices.

“Buyers looking in these pockets of the Northeast should be prepared for a very different experience than buyers elsewhere in the country,” said Redfin Senior Economist Asad Khan. “They may have less room to negotiate, especially on desirable, move-in-ready homes, and could face multiple-offer situations. If you’re shopping in one of these markets, it’s especially important to know your budget, be ready to act when the right home comes along and avoid getting swept up in a bidding war.”

Seller’s markets are the exception to a national picture that heavily favors buyers.

Nationwide, homebuyers have more negotiating power than at any point in Redfin’s records. There were an estimated 70.5% more home sellers than buyers in the U.S. housing market in October, the biggest gap in records dating back to 2013.

That marks the sixth record-breaking month in a row, and the margin is up from 65.6% in September. The Sun Belt is driving the extreme buyer’s market nationwide, led by huge seller surpluses in Miami, Nashville, TN and Las Vegas.

When sellers outnumber buyers, buyers typically have more negotiating power because they have options. That’s why a market with a lot more sellers than buyers is considered a buyer’s market. We define a market where there are over 10% more sellers than buyers as a buyer’s market and a market where there are over 10% fewer sellers than buyers as a seller’s market. A market where the gap is plus or minus 10% is considered a balanced market.

We estimated the number of buyers using proprietary Redfin data on the typical time from a buyer’s first tour to close of purchase, and MLS data on active listings and pending sales. The estimated number of sellers in the market is simply the number of active listings in the MLS. These estimates are seasonally adjusted and subject to revision. See a more detailed methodology here and view an interactive dashboard here.

It’s only a buyer’s market for people who can afford to buy. High housing costs and economic jitters have pushed many would-be homebuyers to back off in recent years, creating the imbalance between buyers and sellers we see today.

“A buyer-friendly market can help make homeownership a little more attainable, even if it doesn’t solve the affordability challenges that have kept many people on the sidelines,” Khan said. “Sellers are increasingly competing for buyers’ attention, which can translate into price reductions, concessions and other savings. First-time buyers don’t have equity from a previous home to put toward a purchase, which makes saving for a down payment a big hurdle–but they also don’t have to sell a home before they can buy or give up a low mortgage rate.  With a historic buyer’s market and home prices that are finally ticking down after three years of increases, first-time buyers who have been waiting–and can afford today’s costs–may have an opportunity to make a move.”

The gap between buyers and sellers widened in October because would-be buyers retreated, while sellers continued listing their homes, albeit at a sluggish pace.

There were an estimated 895,752 homebuyers in the market nationwide in October, the lowest level in records dating back to 2013. That’s down 2% from a month earlier, the sixth straight monthly decline. Mortgage rates averaged 7.33% in October, up from 6.86% in September, which priced out more would-be buyers.

On the flip side, there were an estimated 1,526,984 home sellers in the market, the most since March 2020. That’s up 0.9% month over month.

The number of homes on the market is piling up because many homes are lingering on the market for months without going under contract. Each month, more new listings are adding to the big pool of supply.

Some homeowners keep listing their houses despite falling demand because many have given up on waiting for lower rates. They may want to sell now because they’re worried prices could decline, and some need to move for a new job, a growing family, or another life change. But many homeowners who don’t need to move have substantial equity and little pressure to sell, so they may hold off on listing.

Over 80% of U.S. metro areas, 41 of the 50 Redfin analyzed, are buyer’s markets. That matches September’s record.

For the third month in a row, Miami and Nashville have the biggest surpluses of sellers over buyers. Miami had 195.8% more sellers than buyers in October, and Nashville had 159.5% more. Las Vegas (149.9%) was third on the list. In the top 13 buyer’s markets, there were more than twice as many sellers as buyers in October. Nearly all are in the Sun Belt; four are in Texas and four are in Florida.

The Sun Belt, particularly Texas and Florida, is home to some of the nation’s biggest buyer’s markets, partly because of robust homebuilding. Builders are continuing to add new homes even as demand cools, swelling inventory and giving buyers more options and negotiating power.

Demand has cooled largely because rising mortgage rates and stubbornly high home prices are pricing buyers out. In Florida, rising HOA and homeowners insurance costs are exacerbating the affordability squeeze.

Six metros were “balanced” in October, neither buyer’s nor seller’s markets: Chicago (8.1% more sellers than buyers), Boston (7.5%), Providence, RI (7.5%), New Brunswick, NJ (5.6%), Milwaukee (0.6% fewer sellers than buyers) and San Francisco (6.9% fewer).

The surplus of home sellers over buyers grew month over month in 34 of the 41 buyer’s markets in October.

The surplus increased most in Miami, which also has the biggest surplus of sellers over buyers; there were 195.8% more sellers than buyers in October, up from 160.5% the month before. The next-biggest monthly increases were in Las Vegas, where there were 149.9% more sellers than buyers, up from 122.8%, and Nashville (159.5%, up from 134.3%).

House hunters lost negotiating power in seven buyer’s markets. The surplus of sellers over buyers fell from September to October in Fort Worth, TX (91.6%, down from 100%), St. Louis (23.1%, down from 27.1%), Austin (124.8%, down from 128.2%), Dallas (118.6%, down from 120.5%), Jacksonville, FL, Pittsburgh and Los Angeles. All of them remain buyer’s markets.

As a data journalist at Redfin, Dana Anderson writes about the numbers behind real estate trends. BitGo expands institutional stablecoin minting and redemption platform with USDC

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Clients of BitGo's federally regulated trust bank can use their Go Account to place orders without opening a separate account with Circle.

BitGo BitGo Holdings, Inc. (BTGO) has added USDC (USDC) minting and redemption to its Go Accounts, the digital asset infrastructure firm said Thursday.

The service is available to eligible customers of BitGo Bank & Trust, its digital asset trust bank subsidiary regulated by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.

Clients can initiate and monitor their USDC orders on the BitGo platform or through its API, according to a statement shared with The Block. BitGo will route them through its Circle Mint account, charging flat fees that clients see before they confirm the transaction.

BitGo initially offered minting and redemption for World Liberty Financial's USD1 and SoFi Bank's SoFiUSD when it first launched its Mint service in April, enabling institutions to access supported assets without managing a separate relationship with each issuer.

"The more assets an institution uses, the more work it takes to manage separate accounts and issuer relationships," BitGo CEO and co-founder Mike Belshe said in the statement. "We're building on the infrastructure our clients already use so they can access more assets through one BitGo account."

The service supports USDC on Ethereum, Solana, Base, Arbitrum, Polygon, Avalanche, HyperEVM, Monad, and Aptos, though BitGo said availability depends on jurisdiction and other account requirements.

The onchain components of eligible orders can be processed 24 hours a day, seven days a week, subject to network availability and account controls, according to the firm. However, clients funding or withdrawing fiat will still have to work within the applicable banking processing windows and settlement times.

The news comes after last week's launch of BitGo Global Markets, led by former NYDIG executive Pete Janney, to offer institutional trading and financial services including crypto-backed financing, options, and swaps. That launch followed its acquisition of NYDIG's institutional trading business on Aug. 27. About 30 employees joined BitGo following the acquisition.

Indeed, the two men had worn the colors of Guingamp at the same time, between 1998 and the year 2000. IN 2016, the former defender was sentenced to five years in prison for criminal conspiracy by the Savoie Assize Court.

Including his pre-trial detention and the period following his conviction, Ghislain Anselmini spent approximately two years behind bars. Quickly, he was able to find activity in the world of football. First in the Rhône District, by intervening in training and technical supervision.

I recommend removing your saved payment details, at least for now.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Users of Humble Bundle—the pay-what-you-want game-bundle storefront that will also sell you books and software—have been alarmed by a new redesign that seems to have axed a foundational piece of login security tech.

The just-released new Humble Bundle website has no support for two-factor authentication whatsoever: users are unable to add it to their accounts, while those who had previously set it up on the old site design (including yours truly) are able to log in with just an email and password. At no point in the login process does the site ask you to input a 2FA code.

Humble Bundle does not address the removal of 2FA anywhere on its new site. In fact, the site's FAQs still feature articles encouraging users to make use of it to keep their accounts secure, which is currently impossible. I have reached out to Humble Bundle to ask if the change was intentional and if it will be reversed, and will update this piece if I hear back.

This is, to put it mildly, concerning. The point of two-factor (or multi-factor) authentication is to make user accounts—which, on Humble Bundle, may well have credit card info saved to them—more secure. Even in the event your account password became compromised, your attacker would be unable to get into your account without also having access to your 2FA codes, which are constantly regenerated and only valid for short periods of time. In a world where too many people have bad password hygiene, it's a vital bit of security tech.

Unsurprisingly, Humble Bundle users are aghast at the uncommunicated weakening of their account security. "This is insanely bad, they need to take down password changes entirely until this is fixed", wrote one user in the Humble Bundle subreddit. "I can't even see a way to remove saved payment details anymore, this is seriously dangerous and the site should be taken offline until fixed ASAP", wrote another. They all, uh, they all pretty much go like that. No one is happy about this.

I have to imagine this was not an intentional change. Indeed, I can't fathom the circumstances under which it would be intentional. Either way, while 2FA's absence endures, you might want to play it safe and remove any saved payment details you have tied to your account, though some users report having trouble doing that, too.

Honda’s $2,000 electric moped is a winner. So why can’t Americans buy it?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Honda has been steadily expanding its electric motorcycle lineup, but one of its most interesting new models isn’t a flashy high-performance machine. It’s a modest little electric scooter called the Honda UC3, and with prices now dipping close to $2,000 in local markets, it’s the kind of affordable electric two-wheeler that many Americans would probably love to get their hands on.

Unfortunately, that’s probably not happening anytime soon.

We first covered the Honda UC3 earlier this year when the company announced plans to launch the electric scooter in Thailand and Vietnam. Designed as an electric equivalent to a 110cc gasoline-powered scooter, the UC3 is built for practical everyday transportation rather than weekend joyrides.

But don’t let its modest appearance fool you. The UC3 packs a 6 kW (8 hp) electric motor capable of reaching approximately 50 mph (80 km/h), making it more than capable of handling typical urban commuting.

Its 3.2 kWh lithium iron phosphate (LFP) battery provides up to 76 miles (122 km) of range under WMTC testing. Unlike Honda’s other electric scooters that use removable batteries, the UC3 has a permanently installed battery that charges directly on the bike.

There’s also a full-color TFT display, smartphone connectivity, three riding modes, reverse gear, and a generous 26-liter under-seat storage compartment.

And perhaps most importantly, it comes from Honda, a company with decades of experience building reliable, affordable motorcycles.

The UC3 originally launched in Vietnam with prices starting around 78.5 million Vietnamese dong, or roughly $3,000. However, recent reports from Vietnamese dealerships show incentives bringing prices down to around 54 million dong, equivalent to approximately $2,100.

That’s a pretty compelling price for a 50 mph (80 km/h) electric scooter from one of the world’s largest motorcycle manufacturers.

But despite its appeal, Honda hasn’t announced any plans to bring the UC3 to the United States.

In much of Southeast Asia, scooters are everyday transportation, with millions of riders using them for commuting, shopping, and practically everything else. In the US, motorcycles are much more commonly recreational vehicles, and the market for small, practical scooters is comparatively tiny.

Add in the costs of US regulatory certification, establishing parts and service support, and navigating a market where many buyers expect motorcycles to handle highway speeds, and the business case becomes considerably less attractive.

That’s unfortunate, because the UC3 looks like exactly the kind of electric two-wheeler that could make a real difference in American cities.

It’s affordable, practical, fast enough for most city streets, and backed by a manufacturer that isn’t going anywhere.

Now if only Honda would give Americans a chance to buy one.

Crystal Palace sack Pierre Sage and in advanced talks to appoint Thomas Frank

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Sage goes after 16 games with club bottom of table

He is the favourite to take over at former club Lens

Pierre Sage has been sacked as Crystal Palace’s manager after 16 games, with Thomas Frank in advanced talks to succeed him.

The Frenchman, appointed in mid-June to replace Oliver Glasner, has gone after Sunday’s 3-1 defeat by Liverpool left his team bottom of the Premier League. Frank, out of work since his dismissal by Spurs in February, has emerged as the main candidate to succeed Sage. He spoke to Palace in the summer and is keen for a new challenge after a successful spell at Brentford. It is hoped that he will take charge before next Saturday’s trip to Coventry.

The decision to sack Sage was made on Sunday evening but negotiations over compensation for the rest of his three-year contract delayed his exit. A further complication was that Sage has emerged as the favourite to take over at his previous club, Lens, after they sacked Yannick Cahuzac on Tuesday. The Lens owner and president, Joseph Oughourlian, has remained in close contact with Sage and is understood to have held talks about a return. That would mean Palace could avoid paying some compensation to Sage, having spent more than £4m to buy him out of his contract when he replaced Oliver Glasner in the summer.

Palace, who confirmed that Sage’s backroom staff would also depart, said in a statement: “The club would like to place on record its thanks to Pierre and his staff for their efforts during their time in south London, and wish them well for the future. An update on the process of appointing a new manager will be provided in due course.”

Sunday’s defeat was Palace’s fifth in an eight-match run that also included a painful 5-0 hammering by their arch-rivals Brighton. Their only victory in the sequence came in the Carabao Cup at Fulham and they have lost four league fixtures in a row.

Palace have seven points from 10 games, their league victories having come against Fulham and Nottingham Forest, and have conceded the most goals in the division: 24. They have been juggling domestic matches with the Europa League, where they sit 21st in the 36-team table.

Sage signed a three-year contract after leaving Lens, where he won the Coupe de France and qualified for the Champions League in his final season.

Alternatives to Frank include the former Nottingham Forest manager Sean Dyche, who also has his backers at Palace, and Vítor Pereira, another former Forest manager.

Palace have lost their two main central defenders this year, Marc Guéhi moving to Manchester City in January and Maxence Lacroix to Chelsea in the summer. Two loan players – Axel Disasi and the teenager Honest Ahanor – were signed as replacements. Palace did not land one of Sage’s top targets, the winger Malick Fofana.

Sage is understood to have fallen out with the board over their failure to sign what he regarded as adequate replacements for Guéhi and Lacroix. The Palace chair, Steve Parish, was unhappy with Sage’s treatment of Jaydee Canvot, another centre-back, after the France Under-21 international was dropped from the squad for September’s home defeat by Ipswich, and expressed displeasure after Adam Wharton was substituted in that match.

Why Are My Houseplant Leaves Dropping Off in November?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

This Clever Chopstick Trick Shows When Houseplants Need Water – No More Guessing or Soggy Soil

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Find out what's causing your precious plant to forgo its foliage, and when it's a cause for concern.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Houseplants bring so much life and joy to the indoors at this time of the year, so it can be especially distressing to see leaves dropping. The occasional drop of one or two older, spent leaves isn’t a big deal, but if you see green or yellow leaves falling or an excessive leaf drop, you have cause for concern.

Don't panic, though. Fall leaf drop in houseplants doesn’t necessarily mean they’re dying, but it is a sign of distress that you should heed. The transition to fall and winter can be difficult for houseplants, who can struggle to survive changes in lighting, temperature, humidity, and more. Tropical houseplants are particularly susceptible to these shifts. Environmental changes can trigger plants to drop their leaves, and while some simply need an adjustment period to recover, you can speed their journey back to full health by working out what they're particularly unhappy about.

So, great work getting here to Garden ER where you'll find straightforward solutions to treat this season’s biggest planty problems. Right now, let's identify the kind of leaf drop you’re seeing and the probable cause, and what you should do to set it right. Come on in and let’s diagnose and treat your houseplant with fallen leaves.

Normal, occasional leaf drop happens when an older leaf, usually near the base of the plant, has yellowed or browned and is ready to drop and make room for new growth. Unusual and more concerning leaf drop shows some characteristic signs:

You might see one or more of these symptoms in your houseplant, and they'll help you identify the cause.

Many of the causes of excessive leaf drop are related to seasonal changes in growing conditions. Observe the symptoms in your plants and the conditions where they’re growing to decide which one, or more than one, of these issues is at play.

While indoor temperature shifts right now might not seem that drastic to you, they can be for houseplants. As the temperatures drop outside, your plants’ locations near windows might be significantly colder than other areas of a room, even with the heat on. They might also be experiencing drafts that were absent in summer and early fall. Colder overall temperatures and changes in temperature throughout the day can both be damaging.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Some signs a plant might be dropping leaves due to temperature shifts include suddenly dropped green leaves, leaves falling after a cold night, or leaf drop on the side of a plant facing a cold draft or window.

Tropical houseplants, and in particular ficus – including fiddle leaf fig, weeping fig and rubber tree plant – are especially sensitive to temperature changes, and are notorious for dropping their leaves after even a subtle shift.

If you’ve had the windows open to enjoy the soft breezes of summer and early fall, closing them up in November can be a shock for houseplants. Turn on the heating, the humidity in the house naturally drops, leaving your plants to grow in dry, stale air.

Low humidity stresses many houseplants and in particular that come from humid tropical or subtropical locations such as Alocasia and Boston ferns. Calathea plants are especially likely to develop dry, crispy leaves that drop. In the dry air, these plants lose more water through their leaves than the roots can replace, and this results in drooping foliage, brown and crispy tips and edges, curling leaves, and increased leaf drop. It’s important to note that dry air can affect plants this way even if the soil is moist.

Late fall and winter bring shorter days and less natural light for plants. This means plants engage in less photosynthesis and grow more slowly, and some go dormant, and leaf drop is a common response. In scientific terms, the lower light conditions accelerate abscission, the natural process that causes plants to shed their leaves.

Stress in the environment can also trigger certain hormones that cause leaves to detach before they’ve reached the end of their natural lifespan. It’s a response that helps a plant reduce the demands of maintaining healthy leaves when conditions aren’t favorable, to help ensure survival. So, while some seasonal leaf drop can be expected at this time of the year, an excessive loss of leaves likely means a stress-related acceleration of the process.

You might be seeing abscission due to lower light levels if plants drop a lot of older, yellow leaves at the base and without other signs of issues, like over- or underwatering. You might also see an overall thinning of the plant and less new growth.

Reduced light levels and slower growth means plants don’t use as much water this time of year, and if you’re watering them like it’s still summer, they're likely overwatered, triggering leaf drop. This is a common reason why houseplants develop yellowing leaves in November. When waterlogged roots start to rot, they can’t absorb nutrients to send to the leaves which consequently turn yellow and drop off. You'll also likely see consistently wet soil, soft stems and mushy roots, and you may notice a sour smell.

Underwatering can be an issue too. If the soil fully dries out for long periods, you might see drooping, curling, or crispy leaves that readily drop off.

Plants that live indoors all year are susceptible to subtle environmental changes in November, but houseplants that spend the summer outside and the winter inside can experience even greater shifts. The change from fresh air, sun, and warmth to drier air and less light indoors is particularly stressful, and they can drop their leaves as a result.

Your plants can also be stressed if you move them to different locations within the home. Fiddle leaf figs are particularly sensitive to being moved once they’re comfortable. Most plants will recover from the shock of moving as long as you provide the right conditions in their new spot.

Environmental stress is the most typical reason for houseplant leaf drop, but be sure to check for pests too. Spider mites are common at this time of the year and an infestation can cause yellowing and leaf drop, especially in warm, dry air when numbers rapidly increase. If you spot fine webbing on the undersides of the leaves and where foliage meets stems, that's a sure sign of spider mites.

Watch out for mealybugs, too, which look like white, cottony lumps often in the joints between leaves and stems. Scale insects appear like small brown bumps on stems and leaves, and can also cause leaves to yellow and drop.

Take action to correct these causes and minimize leaf drop, and houseplants will quickly recover. While some of these steps will be particularly important to put right whatever is the cause of your houseplant's stress, all will help a struggling, stressed plant to recover.

Plants don't respond well to being moved frequently so, rather than continually moving your houseplant around to find a location that suits, do some prep first. A basic thermometer that shows the minimum and maximum temperatures reached like this TempPro Digital Thermometer from Amazon will quickly show you exactly how cozy – or otherwise – a particular spot is. Houseplants prefer stable temperatures and it can be surprising what spikes are caused by heating vents and radiators, and what lows occur near doors, in drafts and on windowsills.

Dry indoor air is a major issue for tropical houseplants native to warm, humid areas. Grouping houseplants together naturally increases humidity around them, and you can easily raise moisture levels further with humidity trays. These are simply shallow trays filled with pebbles that sit beneath the pot, topped up with water that evaporates to increase air humidity. A Bonsai pebble tray such as this from Lowe’s is made for the job, or fashion your own with a plant saucer and pea gravel like this Landscape Rock from The Home Depot.

Another option is to use a humidifier and a portable design like the Homedics Humidifier, available from Amazon, is small enough to sit discreetly in among a group of houseplants.

Instead of relying on guesswork, spend $10 on some simple, no-batteries-required moisture meters like these from Amazon, that tell you when the soil needs some more water. Instead of watering on a set schedule, check the soil on a set schedule instead and you'll naturally adjust irrigation levels in line with houseplants' seasonal needs. Make sure all your plants are in pots with a drainage hole and empty saucers or cachepot after watering to be sure roots aren’t sitting in soggy soil.

If you ever suspect overwatering, always check for root rot by sliding off the pot to check for discolored or mushy stems and a foul smell.

Because of reduced daylight hours and lower position of the sun in the sky through fall and winter, pay extra attention to whether houseplants are getting enough light. East-, west- and south-facing rooms are usually best, and rotate plants to ensure they get light evenly. If there isn't enough natural light in the spot you want to display your plants, use a grow light. These needn't be expensive, as this basic dimmable LED grow light such as this from Walmart proves.

If you see pests or signs of pests, deal with the issue right away and isolate affected plants from others to avoid the infestation spreading. Wipe pests, webbing, honeydew and other residue from leaves with a soft cloth, and remove any foliage that is very badly damaged. Finally, if necessary, use an insecticidal soap or neem oil like Captain Jack's Neem Oil Spray, available from Amazon, to manage a pest infestation.

Some plants like caladiums and elephant ear go dormant this time of year, so losing leaves in November is a natural and normal part of their lifecycle. As they're not actively growing, they're better placed in a cooler but not cold part of the house and watering significantly reduced.

End rot is a common challenge for winter squash in fall but identify the cause and it's simple to rescue the remaining crop.

A lost leaf or two doesn’t spell disaster for a Christmas cactus in fall but you can't afford to ignore this distress signal.

Chafer grubs have devastated drought-stressed lawns this year but are easily treated if catch this crucial fall window.

EU Assembles Task Force To Counter Russia

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Home » Regulations » EU Assembles Task Force To Counter Russia

The EU announced two initiatives to bolster the bloc’s sanctions toolbox with enhanced global presence.

The European Union’s (EU) fresh crypto-focused task force is set to tackle top sanction evading challenges, mostly ascribed to Russia. Commissioner Maria Luís Albuquerque announced this to the public following the tenth meeting of the High-Level Expert Group on Union Restrictive Measures.

After the gathering in Brussels, this new EU regulatory arm aims to close gaps that allow Russia to sustain economic activity amid ongoing sanctions linked to its Ukraine invasion. The meeting brought together senior representatives from EU countries, the European Commission, and the External Action Service.

INTEL: EU sets up crypto task force to stop Russia dodging sanctions with digital assets

The examination of emerging financial threats revolves around crypto currency as a safe haven for tax or sanction evasion, keeping a strong commitment to Ukraine. Complementing the task force represents a broader dedicated sanctions office within the EU Commission. This improves coordination across member states.

The G7 is taking a more unified stance across policy, outreach & implementantion. It builds on 21 successive EU sanctions packages on Russia that have gradually targeted crypto-powered channels. Over the next 12 months, intelligence sharing and support from Ukraine and the UK will be used  to identify regular circumvention methods.

The latest sanction package, penned last Summer, freshly imposed transaction bans on 14 third country crypto platforms, enabling potential full bans on crypto services as well. By focusing on detection, enforcement consistency & international cooperation, the EU seeks to limit Russia’s ability to maintain economic resilience, still prevalent even after a flurry of sanction packages.

EU Launches Crypto Task Force to Crack Down on RussiaThe European Union has established a dedicated Crypto Sanctions Task Force to tackle sanctions evasion through digital assets.Announced on November 6, the program seeks to address the means through which Russia can evade… pic.twitter.com/uYJkWjr0Ez

Meanwhile, Russia recently implemented a new digital-asset focused law, lifting transaction & investment bans for retail customers that pass a special knowledgeability test. For businesses, the landscape has shifted too: Russian currency exchanges are able to legally offer crypto services from November 22, 2026, abiding by the legal framework from the local bank authority.

With the geopolitical situation evolving, cross-border transactions powered by the blockchain is seen as a seamless way to lift sanctions & off-day barriers, but it also facilitates a gray area of liquidity for military-industrial circumvention, off-record energy exports & often shadow fleet operations. To mitigate the risks, the EU’s new task force launched new enforcement measures.

Likely someone safe and internal in the short-term, but the doors are wide open in the long-term.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

It was bad enough for Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang that he admitted to having an affair with someone, who people believe to be someone suspected of being a Chinese spy. But it's now come to light he has reportedly had a second affair with a member of Nvidia staff—Kimberly Powell, VP of healthcare. After this latest story it's looking more likely that Huang could be forced to resign.

The question is what's next for Nvidia if the board today decides his tenure as the longest-serving Silicon Valley CEO should come to an end. Huang has been the image of the company in the eyes of the public for a long time, so whoever steps in to replace him will have a big leather jacket to fill. And whoever it is, it will be a major change for the company regardless.

The doors are wide open for Nvidia in the long-term, so it's difficult to pin down with any certainty who any eventual replacement CEO would be, but in the interim the options are more limited. Here's who we could consider seeing as the new CEO of Nvidia.

The first thing to note is that if Huang does get the boot, Nvidia will need to appoint someone to the position quickly as an interim CEO. As one of the biggest companies in the world and a major player in the AI industry which is keeping large parts of the US economy afloat, trying to quickly mitigate economic concerns will be key. This would likely mean picking someone internal to Nvidia.

Top of the list here is Nvidia CFO Colette Kress. If you've listened to Nvidia earnings calls, you'll have heard Kress talking to investors about how the company's doing. Kress could be a likely pick for interim CEO because she's known to investors and heads up company finances. In the short term, to investors she'd seem a safe bet to keep the company in good financial standing amidst any damage Huang's controversies may have done to Nvidia's long-term future.

Other options internal to Nvidia aren't out of the question, either. Jeff Fisher is especially noteworthy because he's very public-facing, but he is very much couched in the world of gaming as SVP of the GeForce department. Kress would still likely be a safer bet for investors.

Chief technology officer, Michael Kagan, is another option, and would potentially be a good fit as co-interim CEO alongside Kress. Kagan joined Nvidia as part of the Mellanox acquisition, a company which he co-founded. He is also a former Intel engineer having managed the design of the Pentium MMX chips.

The PC Gamer Circuit Breaker newsletter breaks down the latest PC gaming tech, and finds you the best deals on the best products

The other Nvidia staff I listed are an option, too. Malachowsky perhaps not so much—he's a co-founder but is very hands-off and maybe doesn't have much real involvement with the company from a leadership position.

Any interim CEO that's picked could be there for a while. When Intel CEO Brian Krzanich resigned in 2018—under vaguely similar circumstances to those Huang finds himself under now—both he and Pat Gelsinger were followed by interim CEOs for months before final decisions were made on their successors. Though in Krzanich's case, the interim successor, Robert Swan, went on to become full CEO for another two years.

In the long-term, the net for Huang's replacement could be cast far and wide. People like Tim Cook and Elon Musk would be pretty outside bets, but with the monopolistic and 'in-bed-together' state of Big Tech in the US, it wouldn't be out of the question. Remember that Nvidia is the biggest profiteer of the AI boom by far, so an offer would be enticing.

AMD's Lisa Su would be an interesting choice, too, and has been touted by a number of people. She certainly has a combination of GPU and AI engineering prowess alongside general public trust and goodwill. In fact, I can't think of many tech CEOs that the public hate less (an increasingly relevant metric, these days).

Then there is also the consideration of who would want to take the job. Sure, it would guarantee a big pay check, but it's a big role to fill with Nvidia's current very flat hierarchy, and any CEO is going to have to be across everything the company does. It's also a role under intense political and economic scrutiny and with the potential for an AI pop still on the horizon, the company might be sitting on a precipice.

Of course, the board of directors could throw us a wild card, and on that front your guess is as good as mine. Though one thing is very likely, whoever comes in is probably not going to have PC gaming much in their thoughts.

Sure, The Last of Us and Uncharted are baked into Naughty Dog’s DNA at this point, but hearing the name Jak in the big 2026 is a very nice surprise.

How and When to Cut Back Black-Eyed Susans – Get the Timing Right for Gorgeous Flowers Next Year

Should you tidy up fading stems or leave them standing? The answer depends on plant health, your garden's appearance, and the birds that visit.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Black-eyed Susan (Rudbeckia spp.) is a popular native perennial, loved for its cheerful, daisy-like flowers with bright yellow petals and contrasting dark centers. As a native flower, this perennial is generally easy to grow, especially once established in the right conditions

One aspect of black-eyed Susan care that can be confusing for gardeners is pruning. Should you deadhead the flowers? Should you cut plants back all the way in winter? How do you trim black-eyed Susans? The answers to these questions, and more, depend on your goals, the time of year, and the types of Rudbeckia.

In our expert guide, we explain when and how to trim and prune black-eyed Susan and answer gardeners’ most frequently asked questions. Careful and timely pruning will keep your plants healthy and coming back year after year.

The timing for pruning black-eyed Susan depends on the reason for doing so. There are a few opportunities throughout the year:

Even within these parameters, gardeners have some wiggle room. You don’t have to deadhead, for instance. You also don’t have to cut back healthy plants just because the growing season has come to an end.

Exactly how you’ll cut back your plants depends on the specific task, but these are general best practices for routine pruning:

Trim off any diseased-looking plant material whenever you see it and dispose of it in the waste, not compost.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Deadheading is one of the most useful pruning chores gardeners have at their disposal. Selective removal of spent flowers keeps perennials like black-eyed Susans flowering until seasonal and weather changes start to trigger dormancy.

Remove spent flowers once the petals fade and drop off, or whenever they no longer look tidy or at their peak. Cut the stem under the flower back to the next healthy leaf or side bud.

You can also accompany summer deadheading with a light overall trim if the plant is looking leggy. Trim stems back to side buds or the base, but do so carefully and selectively. Avoid heavy pruning in summer.

As long as the plant looks healthy, you can keep your black-eyed Susan standing in fall and through winter. The standing seedheads provide valuable food for birds in winter and offer some visual interest in winter beds. The plants also provide shelter for beneficial insects.

If your plants don’t look healthy, for instance if you see signs of fungal disease, it’s best to cut them back. Cut all the stems and foliage back to the ground and dispose of it. Don’t put it in the compost pile, as this can spread disease.

You can also cut back plants if you prefer a tidier appearance in winter. This can also help you prevent self-seeding if you don’t want your black-eyed Susans to spread.

Pruning needs are generally similar across types, but there are a few considerations for different black-eyed Susans:

Deadheading black-eyed Susans encourages more blooming in the same season. Severely cutting back the plant before it has gone dormant can reduce blooming. Cutting back before winter will not restrict next year’s flowers.

For deadheading, cut back to the next healthy leaf or side bud. For seasonal cut-backs, trim stems all the way down to the ground.

Deadheading is optional, but plants should be cut back all the way either in late fall before winter or in spring before or just as new growth emerges.

Not necessarily. Leaving native perennials in place over winter supports wildlife and offers some visual interest in garden beds. The exception is diseased plants. These should be cut back.

Black-eyed Susan pruning is actually pretty simple. Deadhead in summer to enjoy more flowers and cut back spent material before winter or in early spring. The choice is yours and depends on your preferences, type of plant, and whether or not you support native wildlife.

Rangers transfer hurdle emerges for both West Ham & Wolves with Ryan Naderi

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

West Ham United and Wolverhampton Wanderers are looking to bolster their forward options this January, as the Championship promotion hopefuls look north of the border for a Czech goal machine.

West Ham and Wolves are preparing for the January window, and have both seemingly earmarked their centre-forward areas as one that requires some improvement, with Rangers striker Ryan Naderi entering the Championship duo's radar.

Naderi has been in excellent form for the Glasgow-based outfit this season, and could be set for a big money move to England this winter, though West Ham and Wolves seemingly face an uphill battle to secure his services with Rangers not willing to sell.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

According to TEAMtalk, both West Ham and Wolves have registered an interest in Czech striker Naderi, with the 23-year-old enjoying an excellent campaign under Derek McInnes at Rangers.

Having spent time coming through the ranks at Dynamo Dresden and Borussia Mönchengladbach in Germany, Naderi propelled himself into the spotlight with Hansa Rostock, before eventually earning a move to Rangers just under 12 months ago for an initial fee of £4.3 million, which could eventually rise to £5.2 million.

In 17 appearances across all competitions this season, Naderi has scored nine and assisted six, battling with Kevin Kelsy, Lawrence Shankland, and Bojan Miovksi for a starting berth at Ibrox with Youssef Chermiti out with a long-term injury.

TEAMtalk reports, however, that Rangers have no intention of allowing Naderi to go anywhere anytime soon, as West Ham and Wolves will have to pay a sizeable chunk to lure him south of the border this winter - a clear stumbling block for both.

West Ham can boast each of Joel Piroe, Taty Castellanos, and Pablo Felipe as their centre-forward options right now, but despite their obvious quality, recently appointed head coach Scott Parker doesn't appear to be convinced by any of them.

The former Burnley boss will be looking to stamp his authority on the Hammers squad this January, and a move for the six-foot-four-inch Naderi poses an attractive option for Parker, who must expect to face strong stubbornness from Rangers in their pursuit.

Wolves, meanwhile, have each of Raul Jimenez, Adam Armstrong, and Sean Neave as options up top, though with Jimenez injured and not getting any younger, Armstrong struggling, and Neave not yet fully trusted, Naderi has been outlined as a candidate that the Black Country outfit could target.

Naderi's natural physicality and goalscoring ability have surely put him on the radar of numerous clubs across Europe, as well as West Ham and Wolves, who must be prepared to splash the cash if they are to lure him to England in the next couple of months.

With three Championship promotions on his CV, the former England midfielder knows exactly what it takes to guide a club out of this division, and is expected to be backed in January with automatic promotion in his sights.

Cesar Peixoto, meanwhile, will be intent on guiding Wolves back to the Premier League at the first opportunity after succeeding Rob Edwards at Molineux in the summer, and seems to be looking for that added spark up top in Naderi.

Whether either of them will be able to lure Naderi to England remains to be seen, though both of their aims have been made abundantly clear.
