West Ham tracking Sheffield United star alongside six Premier League clubs

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Sheffield United midfielder Sydie Peck has been among the standout performers for Chris Wilder’s side this term. The SUFC ace is no stranger to transfer interest, and his displays mean rival clubs are regularly keeping tabs on his situation.

A new report from TEAMtalk states Championship rivals and summer admirers West Ham are among those monitoring Peck ahead of a potential January swoop. Also interested in the 22-year-old are six Premier League clubs: Newcastle United, Sunderland, Brighton & Hove Albion, Crystal Palace, Brentford and Bournemouth.

It is added that the Blades are ‘aware’ of the interest in Peck and hope to keep him, but there is an ‘increasing expectation’ that exit opportunities arise in January. The England youth international is currently under contract until 2028, and separate reports have said that a new deal is being eyed.

Peck’s impressive displays have seen his stock surge. He’s a regular in the England U21s setup and his hat-trick against Preston North End showed he has potential to add goals to his game from midfield too.

It means that belief in his ability to step up to the Premier League is growing, and Sheffield United’s uphill battle to break into the Championship promotion fight may well speed up the process. However, Peck himself is in no rush just yet.

Asked about his future after summer transfer speculation, the SUFC star expressed a desire to repay the faith shown in him by Wilder and the first-team and academy coaching staff at Bramall Lane. Speaking to The Star in August, Peck said:

“I joined when I was 16 and the gaffer’s given me every debut since. From Premier League to Championship and in the cup. The staff as well, Mickey Collins. I’ve got a lot of stuff to pay back. They’ve helped me become the player I am today.”

Those comments certainly don’t show a burning desire to move on to a higher level. His loyalty would indicate he has little intention of leaving as early as January too, despite his progress and the growing interest.

Amid this new report and fresh claims from TEAMtalk, Peck’s own words would suggest he is unlikely to exit as early as January.

Peck’s comments aren’t the only reason West Ham could have a tough time persuading him to join. The Hammers spent big on a new midfielder in Arne Engels in the summer, and with all of Soungoutou Magassa, Edson Alvarez, Mohamadou Kante, Tomas Soucek, Divine Mukasa and Lewis Orford on the books, there is not a burning need for another player in this position.

Peck will want to go somewhere he is a regular starter, just like he is at Sheffield United, and that may not be the case with the Hammers.

Furthermore, given his commitment to SUFC, it would be a surprise to see him leave mid-season for a club at the same level. If he is to be prised away from the Blades, it seems more likely that Peck heads for the Premier League.

Bitcoin’s rate-cut hopes face a $5 diesel problem in 2027

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Cheaper crude may not bring the fuel-price relief Bitcoin needs: damaged refineries are keeping the 2027 diesel forecast above $5 a gallon.

The US Energy Information Administration raised its forecast for next year’s retail diesel price by 12%, reducing the fuel-cost relief it previously expected. That leaves less room for falling fuel costs to cool inflation and bring the lower interest rates that could help Bitcoin, even as the agency projects cheaper crude in 2027.

The EIA’s November 10 outlook puts the 2027 US diesel average at $5.03 a gallon, up from $4.49 in its October 6 forecast. That is an upward revision to the same annual forecast, rather than a 12% increase in observed pump prices.

Diesel is still projected to fall from a forecast $5.25 average in 2026. EIA also expects Brent crude to average $86 a barrel in 2027, down from a projected $97 this year. Inputs were finalized November 5, so the outlook does not specifically account for subsequent market events.

Cheaper crude does not guarantee a matching decline in finished-fuel costs. EIA assumes petroleum-product supply constraints will continue through the end of 2027, supporting wider crack spreads: the gap between refined-product prices and crude costs.

The agency attributes elevated refining margins to damaged refineries in the Middle East and Russia, which have reduced product exports. It expects disruptions to be concentrated early next year and constraints to ease thereafter, while warning that unplanned outages and deferred maintenance could disrupt refinery operations.

US refinery crude inputs are forecast at 16.5 million barrels a day in 2027, against 16.7 million this year, despite similar utilization. Running the available refineries hard therefore does not necessarily mean processing more crude.

Persistent fuel costs can reach prices beyond direct energy components. In her November 10 speech, Federal Reserve Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle Bowman warned that higher production and transportation costs could pass into consumer prices and keep core inflation elevated. She favors looking through temporary energy inflation when possible, while watching for broader price and expectations effects.

For Bitcoin, the connection runs through monetary policy and investors’ willingness to take risk. A 2023 IMF working paper found that US monetary tightening depresses a common crypto-price factor through a risk-taking channel, similar to equities. If fuel costs prolong underlying inflation and delay monetary easing, that channel could constrain Bitcoin risk appetite.

There are reasons that risk could fade. Bowman reported recent refined-product price retracement and signs of normalizing margins, and still expected core personal consumption expenditures inflation to move closer to 2% next year. November 9 Fed staff research found stronger post-pandemic oil-to-core transmission, but called the results suggestive and model-dependent; its authors said second-round effects had not yet materialized.

Resident Evil Requiem has been crowned 'Ultimate Game of the Year' at this year's Golden Joystick Awards.

In fact, Capcom did remarkably well, scooping a total of six awards, including 'Studio of the Year'. The awards are mostly decided by public vote, with Requiem gaining 17% for its GOTY gong, ahead of Phantom Blade Zero at 16% and Crimson Desert at 15%.

Angela Sant'Albano, who played protagonist Grace Ashcroft in Requiem, won the award for 'Best Lead Performer', while the game also scooped up the 'Best Game Trailer' award for its short film starring Maika Monroe.

Requiem also won 'Console Game of the Year', while Onimusha: Way of the Sword won 'Best Audio Design'.

Unfortunately, no awards were handed out for first-party Nintendo games. Ocarina of Time, of course, launched too late to be eligible.

Arsenal face January dilemma as Liverpool and Chelsea join Alex Scott transfer race

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have made contact with Bournemouth over a potential move for Alex Scott. Mikel Arteta may have to rethink his transfer plans if competition for the midfielder intensifies.

According to The i Paper, Arsenal, Liverpool and Chelsea have all made exploratory enquiries about the 23-year-old. The clubs want to establish what it would take to persuade Bournemouth to sell. However, none appears close to agreeing to a deal.

Arsenal are believed to be keen on a summer move. Liverpool and Chelsea are more interested in signing Scott in January. However, if they were to make a serious offer, it could be hard to hold out.

Scott has become one of the most exciting midfielders in the Premier League. He is an attractive option for clubs looking to improve their squad going forward due to his age, experience and being a homegrown player.

However, there is some uncertainty around Scott’s future. The England international has turned down two contract offers and is unwilling to sign a new deal. There are 18 months left on Scott’s contract, meaning it is an increasingly important decision for Bournemouth.

Arsenal are interested in Scott with doubts surrounding their own midfield options. There have been reports linking Martin Zubimendi and Mikel Merino to exits.

Scott would give Arteta another option, but Arsenal will have to consider if they want to make a move and give Liverpool and Chelsea an advantage.

Bournemouth are reported to want more than £100m to let him go during the January transfer window.

It would be a big asking price, especially given that Scott has 18 months left on his contract. There are a few reasons why Bournemouth could be holding out for a big fee.

The Cherries had to pay £25m to bring Scott in from Bristol City, who will receive 20 per cent of any future transfer fee. They will also have to find a suitable replacement at the halfway stage.

Reports said Chelsea received a number of offers worth around £70m in the summer, but Bournemouth have rejected claims that a bid of £80m will get them talking.

Scott is believed to be happy to stay at the club until the end of the campaign. Bournemouth are also having a great season under Marco Rose, currently sitting in seventh place in the Premier League with eight straight wins in all competitions.

Arsenal have little to hurry into any deal and could use the summer to negotiate a better deal. This is especially true with Scott’s contract situation becoming more of an issue.

The problem is that Liverpool and Chelsea are keen competitors and could lead to a bidding war in January, leaving Arsenal with little room to negotiate.

Whilst Arsenal have made their interest known, this doesn’t necessarily mean he will be moving anytime soon. If Scott is a genuine target, then they must be prepared to pounce if the situation arises.

EMBT shelters Naples metro station beneath "natural and welcoming" vaulted timber roof

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Spanish architecture studio EMBT has completed the Naples Underground Central Station in Italy, contrasting its modernist surroundings with a wavy timber roof.

The station, which is shortlisted in the Infrastructure and transport category of Dezeen Awards 2026, sits within the Centro Direzionale, a business district in Naples designed in the 1970s by Japanese architect Kenzo Tange.

The 10,000-square-metre building was designed to replace a previously understated metro entrance with a new public plaza that felt more integrated with the surrounding city.

As EMBT founder Benedetta Tagliabue previously told Dezeen during the station’s construction, the curvilinear timber canopy was designed in direct response to what she called the “artificial” feel of the modernist Centro Direzionale.

“Kenzo Tange designed Centro Direzionale, and he built a very Kenzo Tange-type of city with reflective skyscrapers and a piazza, very 1970s, very clean,” she told Dezeen in 2021.

“It didn’t work because it’s so different from the rest of Naples,” Tagliabue continued. “So the idea of making a new station there and connecting with the new subway line was also to try to introduce a piece of nature and a piece of the original part of Naples into this artificial piece of the city.”

The roof of the station was constructed from arches and beams of glue-laminated (glulam) timber, which are partially supported by the existing concrete structure and shelter a floor finished in local Etna and Trani limestone.

According to Tagliabue, the branching columns and supports of these arches were designed to evoke the feeling of “walking through a forest”, while referencing the arched roofs of historic railway stations.

“We wanted the everyday experience of catching a train to feel more natural and welcoming, giving people a sense of being at home in the middle of the city,” she told Dezeen.

“We were also interested in what wood could bring to this particular neighbourhood. There is so much glass and concrete here; introducing timber was a way of bringing back a connection with nature, through its warmth and texture,” she added.

“The roof follows the geometry of the tracks, so something that is normally hidden underground becomes visible in the shape of the building.”

The vaulted canopy not only shelters the station’s ticket barriers and access routes, but extends towards the new surrounding paved plaza on either side, which faces a church to the north and incorporates new planting to the south.

Below ground, the station platforms are lined entirely with striped panels in bold shades of red, orange and yellow informed by Pompeian frescoes. This theme is continued above ground in the form of colourful silhouettes.

“Our station is not directly above archaeological remains, so we introduced our own references to that history, with a little humour. The reds and yellows recall Pompeii, and figures appear and disappear across the surfaces,” Tagliabue explained.

“I like the idea that a station can offer these unexpected encounters: along the way, you discover something about the city and feel more connected to it,” she added.

Elsewhere in Naples, sculptor Anish Kapoor and architecture studio AL_A previously designed two sculptural station entrances – one in weathered steel and the other in aluminium – as part of a project to connect the underground to the city’s Traiano district.

Other metro station architecture recently featured on Dezeen includes the mirrored Qasr AlHokm Metro Station in Saudi Arabia by Snøhetta and the series of buildings created for Melbourne’s Metro Tunnel by studios RSHP, Hassell and WW+P Architects.

Aer Lingus Pilots Wanted A Safer Place To Land After Bomb Threat, Now Face Criminal Investigation

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Aer Lingus pilots responding to a bomb threat were directed to land at a French military airfield. According to their unions, they lacked the charts and operational information needed to land safely and requested another airport. They ultimately landed at the base, but then faced questioning and now a criminal investigation over their initial response. I find that troubling, particularly if their supposed failure to cooperate was an attempt to protect the people onboard.

As One Mile At A Time highlights, the handling of a recent Aer Lingus diversion has prompted a pointed warning from European pilot unions.

The incident occurred on November 5, 2026, aboard Aer Lingus flight EI402 from Dublin (DUB) to Rome (FCO). Following a bomb threat, the Airbus A320 diverted to Évreux-Fauville Air Base in Normandy under a French fighter escort. No explosives were found, and the aircraft eventually returned to Dublin.

But the dispute over how it reached that airfield deserves a closer look.

In a November 10 joint statement, the European Cockpit Association, Irish Air Line Pilots’ Association, and French pilot union SNPL said the crew did not have the necessary aeronautical charts and operational information for the military airfield. According to the unions, the pilots requested a more suitable alternative, but that request was refused.

The organizations also expressed concern that the pilots subsequently faced possible criminal prosecution over decisions they say were intended to protect those onboard. ECA Vice-President Captain Paul Reuter put it succinctly:

“The pilots must not find themselves treated as a threat by the very authorities whose assistance they require.”

That strikes me as a very reasonable starting point.

French authorities described the initial exchange differently. The prime minister’s office said fighter aircraft were dispatched because the pilot had not cooperated as expected.

Évreux public prosecutor Rémi Coutin told The Irish Times that investigators were examining whether the pilot initially refused the landing instruction and whether that conduct could constitute a criminal offense. Both pilots were questioned and permitted to leave France. The prosecutor said on November 6 that the investigation remained ongoing.

Aer Lingus has defended its crew, saying they “acted impeccably and were thoroughly professional.”

So we have competing descriptions of the same exchange. An investigation has been opened, but no criminal charges have been filed. I would be interested in what the cockpit recordings and communications with authorities actually reveal.

It is understandable why authorities might want an aircraft carrying a suspected explosive brought to a military installation rather than a busy passenger airport. There may have been very good reasons to choose Évreux.

But those reasons need to account for whether the crew can safely get the aircraft onto the ground there.

The objection described by the unions concerns missing operational information. That is a substantial concern, even if the runway itself can easily accommodate an A320. Pilots still need to understand the approach and assess the conditions for their particular aircraft (I realized this myself when trying to land at Madeira Airport in the United Airlines 737 flight simulator).

A fighter escort cannot make those calculations for them.

I would not expect a captain to continue toward Rome simply because diverting was inconvenient. But requesting an alternative airport because you lack information needed for a safe landing sounds like precisely the sort of judgment I want the person flying my aircraft to exercise!

If the authorities had a compelling reason why no alternative was acceptable, that should have been explained to the pilots and more precise guidance given for landing at the diversion airport. I have trouble seeing how the prospect of prosecution helps anyone land more safely.

I recently wrote about European pilots reporting fatigue and fearing the consequences of speaking up. This is a different situation, but it raises a similar concern: what happens when exercising professional judgment appears personally riskier than going along?

Of course pilots are accountable for their decisions. Their position does not place them above the law, and authorities should examine a disputed response during a security emergency. But there needs to be room for a captain to say that an instruction creates a safety problem. Otherwise, we tell pilots they bear responsibility for everyone onboard while discouraging them from exercising the judgment that responsibility requires.

I also would not dismiss the original threat simply because the search found nothing. I made the same point after the recent Austrian Airlines diversion under a MiG-29 escort. Crews and authorities must respond to the information available at the time and hindsight does not make a precaution foolish.

But taking a bomb threat seriously should include taking the pilots’ landing concerns seriously too.

I cannot resolve this dispute without knowing what was actually communicated between the aircraft and French authorities. Reviewing those communications is appropriate and I hope they are released soon.

Nevertheless, if the union account is accurate, I am firmly sympathetic to the pilots. Asking for an airport where they could conduct a properly informed landing was a responsible response to an already difficult situation.

The bomb threat required a security response. The concern now is whether that response gave enough weight to the safety judgment of the people actually flying the airplane. Those pilots deserve a substantive answer…not prosecution.

Predicting how the USA would line up under Guardiola if dream appointment succeeds

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Has anyone considered how the USA feel in all of this? They’re about to lose one of the most high-profile managers in the history of their men’s soccer team.

Manchester United are hurtling towards the appointment of Mauricio Pochettino at long last. He’s been on their radar for ages and now looks set to get the job.

But United’s gain will be the USMNT’s loss. Pochettino was a big appointment in 2024 in terms of global pedigree after the sacking of Gregg Berhalter and now US Soccer chiefs will have to find a suitable successor.

And according to reports, they’re dreaming big again. Even bigger this time, in fact. They’ve been talking about Pep Guardiola and, according to The Athletic, are likely to place him near the top of their wish list – probably after realising he’s pretty damn good.

It’d be ambitious. Guardiola walked away from management in the summer when he brought an end to his decade in charge of Manchester City. After all he achieved there, and at Barcelona and Bayern Munich before, he’s a GOAT contender when it comes to managers.

He’s earned a break from the touchline, but we wonder if the lower intensity of an international job might just reel him back in.

In the unlikely event the USA were to succeed in appointing him, here’s how we reckon he could set them up.

Guardiola has actually worked with an American goalkeeper before with Man City, but Zack Steffen has only played once for his country in the past four years.

Matt Freese is currently number one for the USA, but the New York City keeper isn’t the most convincing with the ball at his feet.

That’s usually a problem for Guardiola, who wasn’t afraid to bin off a long-serving starter in Joe Hart at the Etihad when he took charge over similar concerns.

He later regretted his treatment of Hart, so might not be so hasty in future, but there are other options waiting to take over in the American setup.

Kochen, currently on loan at Lyngby from Barcelona, is someone who might intrigue him. The 20-year-old has a better ball-playing reputation and will be looking to establish himself as the long-term number one for his country.

No need for a change at right-back. Dest is a pretty easy pick, nearly 50 caps into his international career.

At 26, the PSV and former Barcelona man should be sticking around for the long run.

Richards is someone Guardiola will be familiar with after coming up against him in the Premier League after the defender’s move to Crystal Palace in 2022.

Offering strength and an ability to bring the ball out from the back, he would be a good fit. And he’s already become one of the leaders in the national team anyway.

An even better ball player, McKenzie has a pass success rate of more than 90% in Ligue 1 with Toulouse this season.

At 27, he’s in the prime of his career and continues to thrive in one of Europe’s top leagues.

There were some reports in the tabloids around the start of the decade that Guardiola wanted to sign Fulham’s Robinson for City.

Whether it was true or not, who knows, but there’s no doubting the 29-year-old would still be a dependable option at left-back.

Guardiola would know all about what Robinson can offer from his time in the Premier League.

The current USA captain. Playing at a high level in the Premier League. He’s no Rodri or Xavi, but keeping Adams as a key figure makes sense.

Weston McKennie might be the bigger name, but Musah might be the better fit for a Guardiola team.

He’s improved for AC Milan this season and is well renowned for his press resistance, which is a crucial asset.

His versatility would also appeal to Guardiola, who likes players with the intelligence to cover multiple territories.

Weah largely plays at right-back at club level these days for Marseille, but still plays in the attack for the USA.

Like Musah, his ability to play in various roles would be a big tick for Guardiola. It’s on the right wing he’d probably be best stationed, at least until future Man City prospect Cavan Sullivan is ready for a bigger role.

In the number 10 role, Tillman could be the go-to man. He’s got good control and press resistance, making him a good fit.

Currently playing for Bayer Leverkusen, he has good pedigree after spells with PSV, Rangers and Bayern Munich.

The first place to look for star quality in the USA squad. He was only so-so in the Premier League with Chelsea, but you can’t argue with Pulisic’s record for his country.

He’s been in good form for AC Milan and isn’t going anywhere anytime soon in terms of the international picture.

No Erling Haaland to help Guardiola here. Not even a Sergio Aguero. But there are some decent American strikers he’d be able to pick from.

It’d likely be a battle between Pepi and Folarin Balogun for starting duties up front. Pepi might have the edge thanks to being slightly better at link-up play.

The PSV striker also knows how to thrive in tight spaces in the box and at 23 years old has plenty more goals in his destiny.

Carrick sacked by Man Utd with Pochettino appointment expected

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Michael Carrick has been sacked as Manchester United's head coach, with Mauricio Pochettino expected to replace him.

Rumours had been rife that Carrick may be replaced during the international break, after a dreadful start to the season left United 18th in the Premier League table.

Despite fighting back from 2-0 and 4-2 down to draw 4-4 with Aston Villa on Sunday – the first time any team had recovered from two goals behind to equalise on two occasions in a single Premier League match – United are guaranteed to spend at least 15 days in the relegation zone.

It will be their longest run of successive days inside the drop zone since September and October 1986, when they endured a spell of 26 days in the First Division's danger zone.

That was also the last time United were in a worse position in the table at least 10 games into a season, having sat 20th on that occasion.

Back then, Ron Atkinson was dismissed in November, with Alex Ferguson taking the reins and spending 27 glittering years at the club.

Antonio Conte, Julian Nagelsmann and Eddie Howe had all been touted as possible replacements this time around, with the Red Devils seeking their third permanent boss since Jim Ratcliffe and INEOS took control of the club's football operations in February 2024.

However, United States boss Pochettino is widely expected to be announced as Carrick's successor.

15 – Manchester United are now guaranteed to spend at least 15 consecutive days in the Premier League relegation zone. It is their longest run of days in the top-flight drop zone since September/October 1986 (28). Dejected. https://t.co/wtpy0nCLOx

A club statement read: "After a disappointing start to the season, the club has come to the extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary in order to achieve our ambitions.

"Michael is, and always will be, a Manchester United legend. Just as he did as a player, he has conducted himself with the highest standards of dedication and professionalism throughout his time back with the club. His leadership and achievements last season will always be fondly remembered.

"The club intends to appoint a permanent successor as quickly as possible."

Carrick initially had a hugely positive impact upon replacing Ruben Amorim on an interim basis in January.

He led United to a third-place finish in the Premier League last season, winning 12 of his 17 games at the helm in 2025-26 (70%, D3 L2). The Red Devils also averaged 1.94 goals per game in that span, while conceding 1.05.

Having made Carrick their permanent boss on a two-year contract this summer, United have won three of 15 games in all competitions in 2026-27 (20%, D5 L5), averaging 1.6 goals for and exactly 2.0 goals against per match.

Their 30 goals conceded across all competitions are the joint-second most among all teams in Europe's top five leagues, with only Ligue 1 side Lens shipping more (31, also 30 conceded by Crystal Palace, Sassuolo and Getafe).

United have failed to keep a clean sheet in 10 Premier League games this term, with their only victory coming by a 5-2 scoreline against newly promoted Ipswich Town in August, failing to win any of their subsequent eight matches (D5 L3).

It is the first time they have started a campaign without a shutout in their first 10 matches since that 1986-97 season, while they are the first top-flight team to start a season with such a run, after finishing in the top three the previous campaign, since Liverpool in 1966-67.

Old Trafford was the scene of protests against the way INEOS and the Glazer family have run the club on Sunday, with United's players also attracting fierce criticism from their own supporters throughout the campaign.

Following a 3-0 defeat at home to Bournemouth last month, large sections of the Red Devils' fanbase applauded Carrick down the tunnel, before booing his players off.

120 – Manchester United covered 120.8km in their game against Aston Villa, their most in a Premier League match under Michael Carrick in 2026. Application. https://t.co/qOgNsOZlLT

Carrick, who previously had a short-lived spell as United's caretaker boss back in 2021, ended his second stint in the Old Trafford dugout with a 47% win rate, which is actually the highest of any permanent boss in the INEOS era, ahead of Erik ten Hag's 35% and Amorim's 38%.

United travel to Anfield to face old rivals Liverpool on November 22. A trip to Sporting CP in the Champions League follows three days later, with the Red Devils having six points on the board in that competition, after four matches.

Kane ranks 126th cap among his biggest England moments

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Harry Kane says winning his 126th cap and becoming England's all-time leading appearance-maker will rank among the greatest moments of his career.

England face Croatia in their penultimate group-stage match in the Nations League on Thursday, with head coach Thomas Tuchel already confirming Kane will start the game.

That will take Kane to 126 senior appearances for the Three Lions, surpassing Peter Shilton (125) outright to become the most-capped player in the team's history.

With 91 goals, Kane is already the most prolific player in England's history by some margin, having passed Wayne Rooney's previous record of 53 goals over three years ago.

Since making his England debut in 2015, Kane has put together a case to be the greatest player to ever represent the Three Lions, helping them reach two European Championship finals and two World Cup semi-finals, one of which led to a bronze medal this year.

Ahead of his landmark outing, Kane will also have the honour of presenting the Ballon d'Or trophy to England's fans, having become his country's first winner of the award since Michael Owen in 2001.

Asked where the match will rank among his best England moments, Kane said: "It's definitely up there as the biggest. I think it showcases consistency at the highest level.

"It's testament to all the dedication, the time people don't see, that keeps me fit and healthy.

"This is one of the biggest milestones in my career. In terms of what's next, I don't like to set limits – 100 goals is a milestone to reach as soon as possible.

"We've done extremely well in tournaments, getting to the latter stages. I'm feeling really good and, hopefully, there will be many more years to see what I can achieve."

“It’s one of the biggest milestones of my career.”  The skipper himself on his record-breaking appearance for the #ThreeLions  https://t.co/FiEwBouest

Asked to name some of his other favourite moments in an England shirt, two matches from this year's World Cup immediately sprung to mind for Kane – a 2-1 comeback win over DR Congo in which he scored both goals, and a nail-biting 3-2 triumph over Mexico at the Estadio Azteca in the following round, when his penalty proved decisive.

He said: "The England debut is always a big one. That dream coming true, scoring at Wembley on my debut… coming on for Wayne Rooney will always stick in my mind.

"From the recent World Cup, the DR Congo game was just an incredible experience, to help the team get through a knockout game like that, to score the two goals. The second goal was one of my best finishes and just the feeling of adrenaline afterwards was as good as it gets.

"I've been lucky enough to have some great moments but the major tournament moments, like the Mexico game, they're the ones that really stick in the memory."

And Kane is very clear on what he still wants to accomplish at international level, as he aims to lead England to their first major trophy since the 1966 World Cup.

"To win a team trophy for England, a major trophy, is my biggest dream," Kane said. "A World Cup or the Euros, or both, hopefully, but I know how difficult that is.

"Individual accolades are incredible, but ultimately they're a consequence of trying to make the team better.

"With Bayern Munich, winning the Champions League [is the aim]. There are many trophies I haven't won and that's what motivates me. I'm surrounded by great teams at my club and country, so, hopefully, it's an exciting time ahead."

West Ham Taty Transfer Talks Could Open Door To Goalkeeper Deal

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

According to a number of reports, River Plate are exploring a move for Valentín Castellanos, with an initial loan proposal understood to be one of the options being discussed. The Argentine giants are believed to be assessing whether a temporary agreement could be structured with West Ham contributing towards a portion of the forward’s wages to help make the deal financially viable.

The Argentinian side are also open to exploring a deal in which they would acquire Castellanos while West Ham retain a percentage of his future transfer rights, reducing the upfront fee required.

While the prospect of regular football in South America may appeal to the player, any negotiations are unlikely to be straightforward.

A Taty transfer to River Plate could result in Beltran moving in the opposite direction.

While West Ham are understood to be open to parting ways with Castellanos, the club would want a significantly higher transfer fee than the figures currently being considered by River Plate, creating a sizeable gap between the two clubs’ positions.

Our trusted transfer source suggests it’s unlikely that the Argentinians can afford the striker, as teams over there don’t have any money, yet reaffirmed the striker is very much up for sale.

From West Ham’s perspective, agreeing to subsidise wages while also accepting a heavily discounted transfer fee makes little financial sense unless there are performance-related add-ons or future sell-on clauses that could bridge the gap.

The situation remains at an early stage and there is no indication that a breakthrough is imminent. However, River Plate’s interest appears genuine, and negotiations could continue if the Argentine club can move closer to West Ham’s expectations.

There are also suggestions that Santiago Beltrán, as reported by Gonzo yesterday, is of interest to West Ham, which opens up the possibility of the goalkeeper being included in any deal.

For now, the ball is firmly in River Plate’s court. If they want to bring Castellanos back to South American football, a significantly improved proposal will be required before West Ham consider doing business.

I have been a season ticket holder since the late eighties, so experienced the highs and lows of being a West Ham supporter. I previously wrote for OLAS and have contributed to a number of football publications in the past.

Destiny brought Mauricio Pochettino to Man United - but one huge question remains

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The Argentine has come close to becoming Manchester United manager on three separate prior occasions. Now the stars appear to have finally aligned, but is it too late for either the man or the club?

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

On Sunday evening, Mauricio Pochettino got the call he had been waiting for.

He and long-time assistant Jesus Perez had frequently asked about Manchester United over the last decade. They would often contact friends in the game who were connected to Old Trafford, looking to find out what was happening, whether there could be an opening.

Pochettino came very close to taking one of those openings on at least three occasions: 2018, 2021 and 2024. Now, elements might finally have aligned.

It is a call that others at Old Trafford have long waited for, too. Back when they had a much publicised dinner in 2016, Sir Alex Ferguson essentially told Pochettino he should be the United manager. The Argentine was enthralled at that meeting by stories from one of the game’s great glory eras, and a sense of magic he had felt just standing in Old Trafford had been deepened.

Pochettino had been especially taken by Ferguson’s account of rebuilding United from the 1980s, initially drawing comparisons with what he was doing at Tottenham Hotspur. Now, he’s set to take on that exact same job, if one that has become so much bigger than in 2016. It is arguably even bigger than in 1986.

Pochettino, however, is fired by the idea of being the man to finally fix United. It has become one of the great managerial quests. The United hierarchy have ultimately gone for him because of that personality, and his standing. They don’t want a coach who hasn’t worked at this level. They want someone ready. Both the Glazers and Sir Jim Ratcliffe can sense that, since the two sides of the United ownership have known Pochettino for years.

If this is an appointment that always felt destined, though, that does raise one big question. Is this the right moment for it? Is it like Jose Mourinho, in that it came too late? Is it typical of modern United in that sense? Is it the same old “magical thinking” of hoping a famous name saves them, when the game has changed and the actual problems are now so structural?

Pochettino probably should have gone to United in 2016 or 2018, when he was at a peak in terms of being one of the most exciting coaches in football. The club did consider a move in the summer of 2016, but sources say an exit clause was allowed to expire. How different things could have been.

Old Trafford insiders are now talking about the value of “a name”, but the hierarchy did pass on Pochettino in 2024, when they initially considered sacking Erik ten Hag before ultimately deciding to stick with the Dutchman. His interview wasn’t seen as convincing as that of Thomas Tuchel or Roberto De Zerbi. Those who know him also say he is closer to being “a manager” now, in terms of a wider outlook, rather than the on-the-grass coach that so drove Spurs.

The very fact United are negotiating to pry him from the United States national team, without wishing to be disrespectful, also illustrates how his stock has recently dropped. Some close to Pochettino would nevertheless put that down to necessary choices at certain moments. Taking charge of the hosts of the biggest World Cup ever, that had so much pomp around it, was too enticing. Before that, he probably should have pushed to go to United or Real Madrid in 2018 but he was convinced he could do something special with Spurs. He’d put too much into it.

And that does point to one of the other great twists of fate in modern football, where Pochettino’s profile could be completely different. His Tottenham team were genuinely one of the best in Europe over 2016-18, and one of the great Premier League teams. To achieve 86 points in 2016-17, on what was then only the sixth highest wage bill in English football, was genuine alchemy. It was immense overperformance, fostered by a side that could just overrun you.

We also now know that the wage bill of the eventual champions for that season, Chelsea, had in part come from signings that had been secured by payments against the rules. The punishment from that guilty verdict still looks weak. There is a strong argument that Chelsea should have been stripped of the 2015 and 2017 titles. Pochettino himself said as much in a wide-ranging interview with The Independent and other media in September: “I’ve said that, that we should win one title, one Premier League. We should have been awarded it… for sure, we deserve to be champions for that year.”

Had that justice been done, Pochettino would not just be a “name” who created an exciting Spurs once. He would be an English champion, and one responsible for one of the most historic titles given the much wealthier competition. A great institution would have won just their third title, and first in 56 years.

Both he and Spurs could have had their futures transformed, as well as their place in history. It would have been a modern equivalent of what Ferguson himself did with Aberdeen.

Now, he wants to follow Ferguson in a different way. This is the job he has been waiting for.

A certain apathy towards the US job was displayed in that September interview when he spoke about how his contract didn’t necessarily mean he’d stay the full term.

For all of that, and how their World Cup campaign ended in the chaos of Donald Trump’s disruptive interference in the Falorin Balogun case, there were very impressive spells when the US really looked like a classic Pochettino team. The way they ripped into Paraguay was reminiscent of Spurs 2016-18, right up to some of the flourishes. And that was in a much more limited and restricted environment than a club team.

This is the other thing about just taking the job, too. Regardless of United’s current problems, and perceptions of where Pochettino is in his career, he is energised by this. He has that bite. As one figure who knows him well says, “he has got the IP, he has got the brand, he has got the experience”.

Having already worked for Chelsea and Paris Saint-Germain, he’s been at big clubs with demanding owners. He has the mettle for that. And he now looks set to have the big job he’s always wanted.

We learned in August of this year that mo-cap filming had wrapped, but even then the Last of Us studio was especially tight-lipped about when we might hear anything new about the sci-fi game. Rumors were there thatÂ IntergalacticÂ was aiming for a 2027 release, with unfortunate reports that Naughty Dog employees were being crunched yet again to achieve this, but it’s not until now that these rumors seem more concrete. Scout Motors has 212,000 vehicle reservations as $2 billion South Carolina plant nears completion

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

BLYTHEWOOD, S.C. — Electric vehicle startup Scout Motors has notched roughly 212,000 reservations for its upcoming EVs as the company nears completion of its new $2 billion plant in South Carolina, CEO Scott Keogh told CNBC.

The reservations are for the Volkswagen-backed company's Terra pickup truck and Traveler SUV, which are expected to start full production next year before deliveries begin to customers in 2028.

Reservations do not necessarily translate to sales, as the U.S. automotive industry has learned during the past decade, especially with highly anticipated all-electric trucks such as the Ford F-150 Lightning, Tesla Cybertruck and others that garnered strong interest that didn't lead to meaningful numbers of purchases.

Keogh said he expects Scout could roughly double those reservations by the time customer production begins, but he said the more important thing is to convert early interest into actual sales as production nears.

"It's been a consistent drumbeat," Keogh said during an interview at Scout's plant site outside Columbia, South Carolina. "I think we could easily be up over 400,000 with reservations without doing too much effort. The big thing for us is how much of the initial production can we 100% presell."

Keogh said achieving an order book of 12-to-18 months "would be great" and something he thinks the company can do before production. Scout on Thursday said construction is "in its final phase."

Scout's vehicles will be offered as all-electric versions as well as electric hybrid variants, known as extended-range electric vehicles, or EREVs, that include an engine as a backup generator for the electric motors and batteries.

Scout, a former American vehicle brand from 1961 to 1980, was expected to exclusively offer EVs in a bid for the German automaker to expand its presence in the U.S. However, slower-than-expected adoption of EVs and higher costs led the company to decide to offer EREVs as well.

Keogh said the EREVs and SUV are dominating reservations thus far, with roughly 87% choosing EREV models and 75% being SUVs.

The CEO also said the company is drawing interest from its target markets, which include owners of off-road vehicles such as the Ford Bronco and Jeep Wrangler as well as full-size pickup trucks and U.S. EV leader Tesla

Given the interest signaled by reservations, Keogh said the company is expected to launch with the EREVs first, followed by the EVs. He also said the company plans to launch with mid-level models first, a deviation from many companies that begin with their highest-priced models.

Both the Traveler and Terra are expected to start under $60,000. The EREV models are expected to feature more than 500 miles of range, according to the company, compared with 300 miles of range for the all-electric models.

"The No.1 thing for me is I want to go get the best volume as soon as possible," Keogh said. "In my mind, it's how quick can you get to 100,000 units? It's at 100,000 units where you can start to punch through."

His comments come months after Rivian Automotive received criticism online for launching its R2 SUV with its highest-priced models first, despite that being a common practice across the automotive industry.

Keogh said Scout's South Carolina plant is about 85% complete, but supporting infrastructure and a $300 million supplier park continue to be installed.

Reservations for Scout's vehicles initially opened in October 2024, when it revealed the Traveler and Terra, which feature sleek, yet rugged, exterior appearances that pay some homage to the brand's historic models.

Richard Avedon, Fashion Guru, Does Old Age

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A moving, incisive photography exhibition in Toronto and Montreal explores the way of all flesh.

Left: Richard Avedon, Gloria Swanson, actor, New York, September 4, 1980, gelatin silver print. Center: Richard Avedon, Jacob Israel Avedon, father of Richard Avedon, Sarasota, Florida, October 7, 1969, gelatin silver print. Right: Richard Avedon, The Duke and Duchess of Windsor, Waldorf Astoria, suite 28A, New York, April 16, 1957, gelatin silver print.

© The Richard Avedon Foundation, courtesy of the Museum of Modern Art, New York, Committee on Photography Fund

Old faces — some seem to crease and crumble as we watch — were a specialty for Richard Avedon (1923–2004), the photographer famous for his fashion work for Vogue, Harper’s Bazaar, Revlon, and Elle, magazines not known for plugging geezers. I didn’t know this, so I learned something new in my first minutes in Richard Avedon Immortal: Portraits of Aging, 1951–2004, finishing its tour next month, when it goes to The Image Center in Toronto and the Montreal Museum of Fine Arts. Avedon was a pioneer in presenting cool, stark, tough beauty — he gave us a nude Nastassja Kinski with a boa constrictor licking her earlobe, Brooke Shields in jeans, and lots of movie stars and potentates at their peaks. But old age? Shades of decadence are one thing, but decrepitude?

The Image Center is Canada’s premiere photography museum and part of Toronto Metropolitan University. The exhibition there is similar to Montreal’s edition and displays 100 Avedon portraits. Avedon died more than 20 years ago, and the show spans 50 years, so the subjects are from another era. That said, old age is timeless as well as time-limited. On view are superstars such as Gloria Swanson, Ronald Reagan, the Duke and Duchess of Windsor, and the Dalai Lama. Then there are the period pieces like Dorothy Parker, Oscar Levant, Ezra Pound, and Coco Chanel.

Brian T. Allen is National Review’s art critic.

Elizabeth Warren’s Plan to Abolish Credit Cards

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer (D., N.Y.), and Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D., Mass.) host a roundtable on housing affordability and accessibility on Capitol Hill in Washington, D.C., January 7, 2026.

Elizabeth Warren, never short on annoyances, thinks that interest on consumer credit cards is too high.

The average rate currently hovers around 21 percent. Her solution: Pass a law to cap interest rates at 10 percent. She says she has a bill ready to go, and like-minded lawmakers have signaled their support for controlling rates.

Like any other price ceiling, an interest cap would create a shortage of the product it attempts to make more affordable. With only a tiny fraction of credit cards now offering interest at 10 percent or less, capping rates at that level would force lenders to dramatically restrict accounts — requiring borrowers to put up collateral or limiting access to people with exceptionally good credit — or eliminate them outright.

To progressives, interest on credit cards is nothing but usury — the exploitation of poor borrowers who can’t afford to pay their bills on time. Warren’s sales pitch implies that, under her bill, people would enjoy the same credit just at a lower cost. In reality, almost everyone would see their credit cards disappear.

Regrettably, Warren may also find allies for her proposal among Republicans. She claims she is open to working with President Trump, who called in 2024 for a temporary cap on credit card interest of “around 10 percent.” Senator Josh Hawley (R., Mo.), a right-wing progressive of sorts, recently joined hands with Bernie Sanders to translate that thoughtless musing into legislation.

Interest rates are not some arbitrary number. They are the price for lenders to provide liquidity on the promise, but never the certainty, that it will be repaid. To issue loans through a credit card, banks must first obtain the money by borrowing it themselves, either from depositors or other financial institutions. Interest must be paid on that debt and passed through to cardholders.

It’s true that rates on credit cards, as well as other consumer loans, are typically much higher than those for mortgages and businesses. That is because personal credit is one of the riskiest kinds to extend. Unlike secured loans, repayment depends wholly on borrowers’ creditworthiness. That often fails to hold up: 13 percent of credit-card balances are seriously past due, and approximately 5 percent of debt is never repaid. Higher interest rates exist to compensate for these losses.

Rates also vary based on individual creditworthiness. Borrowers with poor credit history, or people just starting to build their history after leaving childhood or moving to the country, have the most to lose from interest caps. They must accept costlier rates to obtain any credit whatsoever, and if lenders can’t adjust for their added risk, they will simply be cut off. Those in need of funds will instead have to turn to pawnshops or illegal loan sharks.

The credit card industry could always use more competition to keep interest rates at their fair market level. Ironically, the Dodd-Frank Act that Elizabeth Warren championed likely reduced the availability of consumer credit by increasing lenders’ regulatory burden. Warren also despises credit reporting agencies for tracking borrowers’ repayment histories, even though they replaced a far more discretionary and intrusive system in which bankers evaluated applicants’ personal reputations. For someone so appalled by alleged discrimination in finance, she should love objective credit reports.

By and large, Americans like their credit cards and wish to keep them. In trying to rescue consumers from their own financial decisions, Warren would deprive them of their plastic and consign them to infinitely more treacherous alternatives.

The Fight Over Obamacare’s Phantom Enrollees

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The Trump administration is right to take on the problem of improper enrollment.

Administrator for the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services Dr. Mehmet Oz speaks during a press conference on anti-fraud initiatives at the White House campus in Washington, D.C., September 22, 2026.

More than a dozen Democratic senators are criticizing the Trump administration’s efforts to address the problem of phantom enrollments in Obamacare. In a letter to insurers, the senators accuse the companies of “colluding” with the administration to terminate coverage for more than 760,000 enrollees. Their criticism gets insurers’ financial incentives backward and overlooks the extensive safeguards the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services (CMS) employed before terminating these highly suspicious enrollments.

Insurers have little incentive to drop phantom enrollees. For years, insurers have been the biggest beneficiaries of the expanded Obamacare subsidies — reaping tens of billions of dollars in higher subsidies. By making millions of plans free to consumers, Democrats created powerful incentives for improper and phantom enrollment. Given these incentives, the administration was right to scrutinize suspicious enrollments.

Brian Blase is the president of the Paragon Health Institute. He was special assistant to the president for economic policy at the White House’s National Economic Council, 2017–19.

An Intellectual History of California’s Proposition 40

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

How a French academic research project and an elite public university nearly took down the Golden State’s venture capital economy with a wealth tax

People attend a rally in support of Proposition 40 and in opposition to Propositions 41 and 42 ahead of the midterm elections in Los Angeles, Calif., October 5, 2026.

On November 3, California voters narrowly rejected Proposition 40, a 5 percent tax on the net worth of the state’s billionaires. That outcome was not assured. In September, the measure led 52 percent to 46 percent in the Public Policy Institute of California’s survey of likely voters. Had it passed, the state that received 62 percent of all U.S. venture capital dollars in 2025 would have begun taxing founders on the paper value of companies, potentially driving them elsewhere.

The union that sponsored Proposition 40, SEIU-United Healthcare Workers West, supplied the signatures and most of the campaign money. The idea came from an intellectual movement that began with the French economist Thomas Piketty, found an institutional home at the University of California, Berkeley, and received financing from the foundation of a Boston insurance executive.

People attend a rally in support of Proposition 40 and in opposition to Propositions 41 and 42 ahead of the midterm elections in Los Angeles, Calif., October 5, 2026.

Marc Joffe is a fellow at the California Policy Center.

Three Cheers for Gridlock

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Democrats are about to get a stern lesson in the separation of powers.

Senator Bernie Sanders (I., Vt.), President Donald Trump, and Senator Elizabeth Warren (D., Mass.)

Alex Wong/Getty Images, Jonathan Ernst, Evelyn Hockstein/Reuters

The ballots from last week’s midterms haven’t even all been counted and congressional Democrats are already all over your money like Jensen Huang on a sexy Chinese spy.

Senator Bernie Sanders wasted no time in renewing his call for Congress to pass Medicare for All, citing various polls showing that the public favors socialized health care dressed up as a modest Medicare expansion. According to a recent White House Council of Economic Advisers estimate, Sanders’s plan would increase federal spending by $32 trillion over the next decade.
